The embedding in \cref{obj:synth_11} lets the real constants in the rate bounds be compared with honest expected lengths when those lengths are represented in the extended nonnegative-real order. This is the scale used in the final causal length statement.

The main causal conclusion now follows by combining the bridge identity, observed-margin surjectivity, and the observed-data minimax and honest-interval results.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:causal-frontier-lift}[Causal frontier lift]
Let \(J\in\mathbb N\), and let \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\alpha\in\mathbb R\). Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Regime constants.)} The constants satisfy
\[
0<J,\qquad
\beta>0,\qquad
\kappa\geq0,\qquad
L>0,\qquad
c_->0,\qquad
c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+,
\]
\[
p_{\min}>0,\qquad
p_{\min}\leq \frac1J,\qquad
0<\bar\delta<1,\qquad
0<\alpha<\frac12 .
\]
\item \textbf{(Threshold sequence.)} The deterministic thresholds \((\delta_n)_{n\in\mathbb N}\) satisfy
\[
0\leq \delta_n\leq \bar\delta
\]
for every \(n\in\mathbb N\).
\item \textbf{(Split blocks.)} For each \(n\in\mathbb N\), \(B_n=(I_{0,n},I_{1,n},I_{2,n})\) is a deterministic three-way split with pairwise disjoint blocks and
\[
\left\lfloor \frac n4\right\rfloor\leq |I_{0,n}|,\qquad
\left\lfloor \frac n4\right\rfloor\leq |I_{1,n}|,\qquad
\left\lfloor \frac n4\right\rfloor\leq |I_{2,n}|.
\]
\end{itemize}
Let \(h_n\), the information-balance bandwidth for \(\delta_n\), be as in \cref{obj:def:bandwidth}, and let
\[
r_n=n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta
\]
be the frontier rate from \cref{obj:def:frontier}. Then there exist constants \(c,C\in\mathbb R\), with \(0<c<C\), depending only on these regime constants, such that, for every threshold sequence and every deterministic split-block sequence satisfying the preceding conditions, all sufficiently large \(n\) satisfy the following conclusions.

The total-Gram estimator \(\widehat\theta_n\) in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}, formed with split \(B_n\), order \(\ell=\lceil\beta\rceil-1\), bandwidth \(h_n\), and threshold \(\delta_n\), is observed-sample measurable. The corresponding bias-aware interval \(\mathrm{CI}_n\) in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}, formed with the same split, order, bandwidth, and threshold, is an observed-sample measurable interval.

For the causal minimax criteria \(R_{n,\mathrm F}^\star\) and \(L_{n,\mathrm F}^\star\) in \cref{obj:def:causal-frontier-criteria},
\[
c r_n\leq R_{n,\mathrm F}^\star
\leq
\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}}
\mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta_n-\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\right|
\leq C r_n,
\]
\[
\inf_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}}
(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}\{\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\in \mathrm{CI}_n\}
\geq 1-\alpha,
\]
\[
\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}}
\mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}}\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n)
\leq C r_n.
\]
Moreover, in the extended nonnegative-real order, after embedding the nonnegative real bounds into \(\mathbb R_{\geq0}\cup\{\infty\}\),
\[
c r_n\leq L_{n,\mathrm F}^\star\leq C r_n .
\]
\end{theoremv}

\Cref{obj:thm:causal-frontier-lift} transports the observed minimax and honest-length frontier to the causal clamp mean on \(\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}\). The same total-Gram estimator and the same bias-aware interval remain observed-sample procedures, and their guarantees hold uniformly over the full-data class because \cref{obj:prop:causal-bridge} aligns the target while \cref{obj:prop:observed-margin-surjectivity} aligns the induced decision criteria. The rate is therefore the same \(r_n\) characterized in \cref{obj:def:frontier,obj:thm:phase-diagram}: root-\(n\) and boundary-driven regimes carry over with the causal interpretation supplied by the full-data lift.


\section{Continuity-only clamp inference}
\label{sec:continuity-only}

\Cref{sec:setup-assumptions,sec:main-statistical-results} impose a Hölder modulus for the regression at the lower threshold. We now record the enlarged continuity-only experiment, which keeps the same density, stratum-mass, and lower-tail thinning restrictions while replacing the Hölder regression member with qualitative continuity.

\begin{definitionv}{def:continuity-model-class}[Continuity-only model class \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\)]
\(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) is the set of laws
\[
P\in\mathcal P(\mathcal X\times[0,1]\times[0,1])
\]
satisfying \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law,obj:ass:stratum-mass,obj:ass:polynomial-thinning} and the following continuity condition: for every \(x\in\mathcal X\), an almost-everywhere version of
\[
a\mapsto \mathbb E_P[Y\mid A=a,X=x]
\]
admits a continuous extension to \([0,\bar\delta]\). The continuity extension is required memberwise for each stratum.
\end{definitionv}

The class in \cref{obj:def:continuity-model-class} preserves the design restrictions that govern the amount of mass near the lower endpoint. Its regression condition supplies a selected point value at the clamp threshold through continuity, rather than through a quantitative Hölder modulus.

The corresponding target has the same retained-course plus threshold-atom decomposition as \(\theta_\delta(P)\) in \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional}; the atom value is read from the continuous extension in the continuity-only class.

\begin{definitionv}{def:continuity-clamp-functional}[Continuity clamp target \(\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\)]
For \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) and \(0\leq\delta\leq\bar\delta\), with \(q_x(\delta)\) as in \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional} and \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) as in \cref{obj:def:continuity-model-class}, define
\[
 \theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)
 =
 \mathbb E_P\!\left[Y\mathbf 1\{A>\delta\}\right]
 +
 \sum_{x\in\mathcal X}
 p_xq_x(\delta)\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(\delta).
\]
\end{definitionv}

The continuity-only scale is determined by the ordinary sampling fluctuation and the amount of mass moved by the lower-threshold clamp.

\begin{definitionv}{def:continuity-frontier}[Continuity frontier \(s_n\)]
Define the continuity-only frontier rate by
\[
s_n=n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}.
\]
\end{definitionv}

The estimator and interval make the enlarged model explicit. They use the retained-course mean above the threshold and a fixed midpoint value for the lower-tail atom, with a bounded-sum allowance for the empirical lower-tail mass.

\begin{algorithmv}{def:continuity-fallback-estimator}[Continuity fallback estimator \(\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\)]
The inputs are the deterministic split blocks \(I_0,I_1\), the threshold \(\delta_n\), and the observations \(O_1,\ldots,O_n\).
\begin{enumerate}
\item Define the retained-course empirical mean
\[
\widehat\nu_n
=
|I_0|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_0}Y_i\mathbf 1\{A_i>\delta_n\}.
\]
\item For each \(x\in\mathcal X\), define the empirical lower-threshold atom mass
\[
\widehat b_x
=
|I_1|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_1}\mathbf 1\{X_i=x,A_i\leq\delta_n\}.
\]
\item Output the fixed-regression-fallback estimator
\[
\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}
=
\Pi_{[0,1]}\left\{\widehat\nu_n+\frac12\sum_{x\in\mathcal X}\widehat b_x\right\}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{algorithmv}

\begin{algorithmv}{def:continuity-honest-interval}[Continuity honest interval \(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\)]
The inputs are the split blocks \(I_0,I_1\), the level \(\alpha\), the estimator \(\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\), and the empirical masses \(\widehat b_x\).
\begin{enumerate}
\item For \(j=0,1\), define the split-specific Hoeffding radius
\[
t_{j,\alpha}
=
\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_j|}}.
\]
\item Define the empirical total lower-threshold atom mass
\[
\widehat B_n
=
\sum_{x\in\mathcal X}\widehat b_x.
\]
\item Output the Hoeffding interval
\[
\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}
=
\left[
\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-t_{0,\alpha}-t_{1,\alpha}-\frac12\widehat B_n,\,
\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}+t_{0,\alpha}+t_{1,\alpha}+\frac12\widehat B_n
\right]\cap[0,1].
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{algorithmv}

The concentration step for \cref{obj:def:continuity-honest-interval} uses the standard bounded-sum inequality of \citet[Theorem~2]{Hoeffding1963}, applied separately to the retained-course and atom-mass split statistics.

The causal version of the continuity-only analysis uses the same structural ingredients as \cref{obj:def:full-data-model-class}, with the observed margin placed in \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\).

\begin{definitionv}{def:continuity-full-data-model-class}[Continuity full-data class \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\)]
Define \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\) as the collection of full-data law packages \(P^{\mathrm F}\) with a memberwise standard Borel latent-response space \(\mathcal U\), latent variable \(U\), jointly Borel stratum-specific response maps \(g_x\), and jointly measurable potential-outcome process \(\{Y(a):a\in[0,1]\}\), such that:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Observed margin.)} The observed \((X,A,Y)\)-margin of \(P^{\mathrm F}\) belongs to \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) in \cref{obj:def:continuity-model-class}.
\item \textbf{(Consistency.)} The package satisfies \cref{obj:ass:consistency}.
\item \textbf{(Exchangeability.)} The package satisfies \cref{obj:ass:exchangeability}.
\item \textbf{(Response continuity.)} The package satisfies \cref{obj:ass:full-data-response-continuity}.
\end{itemize}
The carrier spaces \(\mathcal U\) may vary with \(P^{\mathrm F}\).
\end{definitionv}

For later comparison, the next definition fixes the observed margin of a full-data law as the pushforward onto the observed coordinates.

\begin{definitionv}{def:continuity-frontier-criteria}[Continuity frontier criteria \(R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\) and \(L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\)]
For estimators and intervals measurable with respect to \(O_1,\ldots,O_n\), define the continuity-only observed minimax absolute-error risk by
\[
R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star
=
\inf_{\widehat\theta}\sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta-\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right|.
\]
Define the continuity-only observed minimax honest expected length by
\[
L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star
=
\inf_{C_n:\,\inf_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}}P^{\otimes n}
\{\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\in C_n\}\geq1-\alpha}
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\operatorname{len}(C_n)\right].
\]
For the full-data continuity-only class, define the causal minimax absolute-error risk by
\[
R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star
=
\inf_{\widehat\psi}\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}}
\mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\psi-\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\right|.
\]
Define the continuity-only causal minimax honest expected length by
\[
L_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star
=
\inf_{C_n:\,\inf_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}}
(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}\{\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\in C_n\}\geq1-\alpha}
\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}}
\mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\operatorname{len}(C_n)\right].
\]
\end{definitionv}

The decision-theoretic criteria are recorded for both the observed continuity-only class and its full-data counterpart.

\begin{propositionv}{prop:continuity-causal-bridge}[Continuity causal bridge]
Let \(J\in\mathbb N\), let \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta\in\mathbb R\), and let \(P^{\mathrm F}\) be a full-data law package with observed margin \(P\).  Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Full-data continuity model.)} \(P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\) with constants \((\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta)\), as in \cref{obj:def:continuity-full-data-model-class}.
\item \textbf{(Design constants.)} \(J>0\), \(\kappa\geq0\), \(c_->0\), \(c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+\), \(0<p_{\min}\leq 1/J\), and \(0<\bar\delta<1\).
\end{itemize}
For each \(x\in\mathcal X\) and \(a\in\mathbb R\), define the full-data response mean by
\[
m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)
=
P^{\mathrm F}(X=x)^{-1}
\int_{\{X=x\}} g_x(a,U)\,dP^{\mathrm F}.
\]
Then, for every \(x\in\mathcal X\) and every \(a\in[0,\bar\delta]\),
\[
m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)=\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(a).
\]
Moreover, if
\[
\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})
=
\int Y\!\left(d_\delta(A)\right)\,dP^{\mathrm F},
\]
then, for every \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\),
\[
\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})
=
\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P),
\]
where \(\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\) is the continuity-only clamp functional in \cref{obj:def:continuity-clamp-functional}.
\end{propositionv}

The first causal statement aligns the full-data response mean with the observed continuous extension and therefore identifies the causal clamp mean with the continuity-only observed target.

\begin{propositionv}{prop:continuity-observed-margin-surjectivity}[Observed-margin surjectivity]*
Let \(J\in\mathbb N\) and let \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\alpha\in\mathbb R\). Suppose that the continuity-regime design constants used in \cref{obj:def:continuity-model-class,obj:def:continuity-full-data-model-class,obj:def:continuity-frontier-criteria} hold for \((J,\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta)\), and that \(0<\alpha<1/2\).

Then the observed-margin map from \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\) onto \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) is surjective: for every \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\), there exists \(P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\) whose observed margin is \(P\). Moreover, for every \(n\in\mathbb N\) and every \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\), the four continuity-frontier criteria of \cref{obj:def:continuity-frontier-criteria}, evaluated at \((J,n,\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\delta,\alpha)\), satisfy
\[
R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star
=
R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star,
\qquad
L_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star
=
L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .
\]
\end{propositionv}
% CAUSALSMITH-CITED-SCOPE-BEGIN prop:continuity-observed-margin-surjectivity
\verificationfootnotetext{\textbf{Formalization scope.} This result uses the published conclusion \citep{Kallenberg2002Foundations} (Theorem 6.3 (conditional distribution)). The cited source proof is not formalized here: Lean verifies its use and all remaining steps. If that cited conclusion is false, the portions of this result that depend on it are not certified.}
% CAUSALSMITH-CITED-SCOPE-END prop:continuity-observed-margin-surjectivity

\Cref{obj:prop:continuity-causal-bridge} shows that qualitative continuity turns almost-everywhere regression information into the pointwise threshold value needed after the clamp collapses the lower tail. Under the stated full-data continuity conditions, the structural response mean agrees with the observed continuous extension throughout the threshold envelope, and the causal clamp mean equals the observed continuity clamp target.

The next statement records that the continuity-only full-data class covers the observed continuity-only class exactly at the margin. Its construction uses regular conditional distributions on standard Borel spaces, as supplied by \citet[Theorem~6.3]{Kallenberg2002Foundations}.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_13}[Observed margin of a full-data law]
For a full-data law package $P^{\mathrm F}$ governing $(X,A,Y,U,\{Y(a):a\in[0,1]\})$, define $P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}$ to be the observed-data marginal law of $(X,A,Y)$. Equivalently, if $\pi_{\mathrm{obs}}(X,A,Y,U,\{Y(a)\})=(X,A,Y)$ is the coordinate projection onto $\mathcal X\times[0,1]\times[0,1]$, then $P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}=(\pi_{\mathrm{obs}})_{\#}P^{\mathrm F}$.
\end{definitionv}

\Cref{obj:prop:continuity-observed-margin-surjectivity} puts the observed and causal continuity-only decision problems on the same footing. Every observed continuity-only law has a full-data representative satisfying the causal restrictions, and the minimax risk and honest expected-length criteria agree exactly for each sample size and threshold in the stated range.

The main continuity-only conclusion now combines the bridge identity, observed-margin surjectivity, and the fallback estimator and interval.

\begin{theoremv}{thm:continuity-only-frontier}[Continuity frontier characterization]*
Fix \(J\in\mathbb N\) and constants \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\alpha\in\mathbb R\). Suppose
\[
0<J,
\qquad
0\leq \kappa,
\qquad
0<c_-,
\qquad
c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+,
\qquad
0<p_{\min}\leq \frac{1}{J},
\qquad
0<\bar\delta<1,
\qquad
0<\alpha<\frac12.
\]
Then there exist constants \(c,C\in\mathbb R\) with \(0<c<C\) such that the following statements hold.

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Uniform frontier bounds.)} For every deterministic threshold sequence \((\delta_n)_{n\geq1}\) with \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\) for all \(n\), and for every deterministic sequence \(B_{\bullet}=(B_n)_{n\geq1}\) of admissible three-way split blocks, all sufficiently large \(n\) satisfy the following bounds. With
\[
s_n=n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1},
\]
and with \(R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\), \(L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\), \(R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star\), and \(L_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star\) as in \cref{obj:def:continuity-frontier-criteria},
\[
c s_n\leq R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star
\leq
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right|
\leq C s_n,
\]
the interval \(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\) has uniform coverage at least \(1-\alpha\) over \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\), its worst-case expected length is at most \(C s_n\), and
\[
c s_n\leq L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star.
\]
Moreover,
\[
R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star=R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star,
\qquad
L_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star=L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star,
\]
and the same estimator and interval satisfy
\[
R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star
\leq
\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}}
\mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\right|
\leq C s_n,
\]
with causal coverage at least \(1-\alpha\) uniformly over \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\) and causal worst-case expected length at most \(C s_n\).

\item \textbf{(Elbow and null-threshold rates.)} For the continuity-only rate in \cref{obj:def:continuity-frontier},
\[
s_n\bigl(\delta_n=n^{-1/(2(\kappa+1))}\bigr)
\asymp n^{-1/2},
\qquad
s_n(0)\asymp n^{-1/2}.
\]

\item \textbf{(Fixed positive thresholds.)} For every deterministic threshold sequence \((\delta_n)_{n\geq1}\) with \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\) for all \(n\), every \(\delta_0\in(0,\bar\delta]\), and \(\delta_n\to\delta_0\),
\[
R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star \not\to 0,
\qquad
L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star \not\to 0.
\]
\end{itemize}
\end{theoremv}
% CAUSALSMITH-CITED-SCOPE-BEGIN thm:continuity-only-frontier
\verificationfootnotetext{\textbf{Formalization scope.} This result uses the published conclusion \citep{Hoeffding1963} (Theorem 2, specialized to unit ranges and two tails). The cited source proof is not formalized here: Lean verifies its use and all remaining steps. If that cited conclusion is false, the portions of this result that depend on it are not certified.}
% CAUSALSMITH-CITED-SCOPE-END thm:continuity-only-frontier

\Cref{obj:thm:continuity-only-frontier} characterizes the continuity-only rate for both observed and causal formulations. The fallback estimator and Hoeffding interval attain the rate \(s_n\), while the minimax risk and honest expected length are bounded below at the same order. The honest-length comparison follows the nonparametric confidence-interval lower-bound phenomenon of \citet{Low1997NonparametricConfidenceIntervals}, specialized here to the threshold-atom decision problem.

The rate has a simple interpretation. When \(\delta_n\) is at the elbow \(n^{-1/[2(\kappa+1)]}\), the lower-tail mass term \(\delta_n^{\kappa+1}\) matches \(n^{-1/2}\), so the continuity-only problem has a root-\(n\) scale. At \(\delta_n=0\), the atom term vanishes and the bounded retained-course mean again gives the root-\(n\) scale. For thresholds converging to a fixed positive value, the theorem records persistent minimax risk and honest length, reflecting the positive amount of treatment mass assigned a threshold regression value governed by qualitative continuity.


\section{Discussion, limitations, and open questions}
\label{sec:discussion-open-questions}

The results characterize how lower-tail overlap and smoothness jointly determine inference for threshold modified treatment policies with continuous treatments. In the Hölder model \(\mathcal M\), \cref{obj:thm:minimax-risk,obj:thm:honest-length} give the common rate \(r_n\), combining the ordinary sampling term \(n^{-1/2}\) with the local extrapolation term \(\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\). The phase diagram in \cref{obj:thm:phase-diagram} explains how this expression changes with the threshold sequence: moving the clamp closer to the lower endpoint changes both the mass affected by the policy and the amount of information available near the threshold.

The continuity-only comparison in \cref{obj:thm:continuity-only-frontier} separates quantitative smoothness from qualitative continuity. With a Hölder radius and exponent fixed in the model, the local polynomial construction uses observations in a shrinking neighborhood of \(\delta_n\) and pays the bias term \(h_n^\beta\). With continuity alone, the delivered rate is \(s_n=n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}\), because the threshold regression contribution is controlled through boundedness and continuity rather than through a quantitative modulus. Taken together, the two frontiers show that atom mass and smoothness enter different parts of the problem: the mass below the threshold weights the regression value to be learned, while the smoothness condition determines how sharply that value can be inferred from nearby continuous-treatment observations.

The causal lifting results give the same statistical rates a full-data interpretation. Under the consistency, exchangeability, and response-continuity conditions in \cref{obj:ass:consistency,obj:ass:exchangeability,obj:ass:full-data-response-continuity}, \cref{obj:prop:causal-bridge,obj:thm:causal-frontier-lift} transfer the observed-data Hölder frontier to the causal clamp mean. The continuity-only bridge in \cref{obj:prop:continuity-causal-bridge,obj:thm:continuity-only-frontier} gives the analogous interpretation for the qualitative-continuity class. These statements make the role of the full-data class explicit: once the observed margin belongs to the relevant observed model and the stated causal restrictions hold, the observed clamp functional and the causal clamp mean have matching minimax criteria.

The exact-modulus perspective below records a more calibrated object behind the order results. The intervals in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval} use analytic upper bounds on local bias and stochastic error. A sharper implementation can instead optimize directly over affine weights in the realized local design, in the spirit of modulus-based honest inference \citep{ArmstrongKolesar2018OptimalInference,ArmstrongKolesar2020}. The next three definitions name the realized affine-modulus handle, the integrated split-specific radius, and the resulting interval used to formulate the sharp calibration question.

\begin{definitionv}{def:exact-modulus-handle}[Exact modulus radius]
With notation as in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation,obj:def:model-class}, fix \(P\in\mathcal M\) under the displayed \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min}\) member conditions, a deterministic split \(B=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) with pairwise-disjoint blocks satisfying \(|I_m|\geq \lfloor n/4\rfloor\) for \(m=0,1,2\), a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\), a local-polynomial degree \(\ell\in\mathbb N\), a threshold \(\delta\), a bandwidth \(h>0\), and a multiplier \(t\geq0\). For a realized observed sample \(z=(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\), with \(O_i=(X_i,A_i,Y_i)\), set
\[
u_i=\frac{A_i-\delta}{h},
\qquad
S_{z,x}=\{i\in I_2:X_i=x,\ 0\leq u_i\leq1\}.
\]
The affine weight set is
\[
\mathcal W_{z,x}
=
\left\{
w=(w_i)_{i=1}^n\in\mathbb R^n:
w_i=0\ \text{whenever } i\notin I_2\ \text{or }X_i\neq x\ \text{or }u_i\notin[0,1],
\quad
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_i u_i^j=\mathbf 1\{j=0\}\ \text{for }j=0,\ldots,\ell
\right\}.
\]
For \(w\in\mathcal W_{z,x}\), let \(B^{\mathrm H}_{z,x}(w)\) denote the exact Hölder bias term from the \((\beta,L)\)-Hölder regression class in \cref{obj:ass:holder-regression}. Define the realized affine-modulus radius by
\[
R^{\mathrm{ex}}_{z,x}(t)
=
\inf\left\{
B^{\mathrm H}_{z,x}(w)
+
t\left(\sum_{i\in I_2}w_i^2\right)^{1/2}
:
w\in\mathcal W_{z,x}
\right\},
\]
with the real-valued convention that this infimum is \(0\) when the displayed feasible set is empty. The integrated exact-modulus radius is
\[
M^{\mathrm{ex}}_{x}(P,B,\ell,\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\delta,h,t)
=
\int R^{\mathrm{ex}}_{z,x}(t)\,\mathrm dP^{\otimes n}(z),
\]
where \(P^{\otimes n}\) is the canonical product law of the observed sample. Both \(R^{\mathrm{ex}}_{z,x}(t)\) and \(M^{\mathrm{ex}}_{x}(P,B,\ell,\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\delta,h,t)\) are radius, or half-length, quantities.
\end{definitionv}

\begin{definitionv}{synth_21}[Exact-modulus radius \(W_{n,B_n}\)]
Adopt the notation and hypotheses of the objects introduced earlier in the paper.  Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\), a deterministic third split block \(I_2\), a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\), and a multiplier \(t\geq0\).  For a realized design \(z=\{(X_i,A_i):i\in I_2\}\), set
\[
u_i=\frac{A_i-\delta_n}{h_n},
\qquad
S_{z,x}=\{i\in I_2:X_i=x,\ 0\leq u_i\leq1\}.
\]
Let
\[
\mathcal W_{z,x}
=
\left\{
w=(w_i)_{i\in I_2}\in\mathbb R^{I_2}:
w_i=0\ \text{for }i\notin S_{z,x},\
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_i u_i^j=\mathbf 1\{j=0\}\ \text{for }j=0,\ldots,\ell
\right\}.
\]
Let \(\mathcal H_{\beta,L}\) be the class of continuous functions \(f:[0,1]\to[0,1]\) satisfying the declared order-\(\ell\) Hölder Taylor-remainder bound with exponent \(\beta\) and radius \(L\).  For \(w\in\mathcal W_{z,x}\), define the realized Hölder bias functional
\[
B_{z,x}(w)
=
\sup_{f\in\mathcal H_{\beta,L}}
\left|
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_i f(A_i)-f(\delta_n)
\right|.
\]
The realized affine-modulus radius at multiplier \(t\) is
\[
\mathcal R_{z,x}(t)
=
\begin{cases}
\displaystyle
\inf_{w\in\mathcal W_{z,x}}
\left\{
B_{z,x}(w)
+
t\left(\sum_{i\in I_2}w_i^2\right)^{1/2}
\right\},
&\mathcal W_{z,x}\neq\varnothing,\\[1ex]
0,&\mathcal W_{z,x}=\varnothing.
\end{cases}
\]
The integrated exact-modulus radius is
\[
\mathfrak R_{n,x}(P,I_2;t)
=
\int
\mathcal R_{z,x}(t)\,
\mathrm d(P_{X,A}^{\otimes I_2})(z),
\]
where \(P_{X,A}^{\otimes I_2}\) is the independent observed-design product law on the coordinates in \(I_2\).

If \(B_n=(I_{0,n},I_{1,n},I_{2,n})\) is an admissible deterministic three-way split at sample size \(n\), its worst integrated exact-modulus radius is
\[
W_{n,B_n}
=
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}\sup_{x\in\mathcal X}
\mathfrak R_{n,x}\!\left(P,I_{2,n};t_\alpha\right),
\qquad
t_\alpha=\sqrt{\log(12J/\alpha)/2}.
\]
Thus \(W_{n,B_n}\) is a radius quantity computed from the third block of \(B_n\), and the associated untruncated symmetric full length is \(2W_{n,B_n}\).
\end{definitionv}

\begin{definitionv}{synth_20}[Exact-modulus interval]
Assume the notation, model conditions, and preceding constructions in the objects introduced earlier in the paper. Fix an admissible deterministic three-way split \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) at sample size \(n\). For a realized observed sample \(z\), let \(\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}(z)\) be the total-Gram estimator formed with the blocks in \(B_n\), and evaluate the realized affine-modulus radius \(R^{\mathrm{ex}}_{z,x}(t_\alpha)\) on the \((X,A)\)-projection of \(z\) using the third block \(I_2\), where
\[
t_\alpha=\sqrt{\log(12J/\alpha)/2}.
\]
Define
\[
R_{n,B_n}(z)
=
\sup_{x\in\mathcal X}
R^{\mathrm{ex}}_{z,x}(t_\alpha).
\]
The exact-modulus interval is
\[
C^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}(z)
=
\left[
\max\left\{0,\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}(z)-R_{n,B_n}(z)\right\},
\min\left\{1,\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}(z)+R_{n,B_n}(z)\right\}
\right].
\]
Its worst-case expected length over the observed-data model is
\[
\ell^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}
=
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left[
\operatorname{len}\!\left(C^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}\right)
\right].
\]
\end{definitionv}

\Cref{obj:def:exact-modulus-handle,obj:synth_21,obj:synth_20} record one concrete, fully specified object against which the sharp-constant question can be posed: an interval centered at the total-Gram estimator whose half-width is the realized affine-modulus radius, intersected with \([0,1]\).  It is important to be precise about what that object does and does not calibrate.  Its radius solves the affine-modulus problem for the threshold-regression component alone: it is the supremum over strata of unweighted realized regression radii, it does not include the retained-mean deviation radius, the atom-mass deviation radii, or the atom weights \(p_xq_x(\delta_n)\) by which those components enter the target, and it does not aggregate across strata in the way the target does.  On the singular realized designs where the affine constraints are infeasible the realized radius is zero, so the object is not by itself a coverage-guaranteed procedure; the honest interval with a proved coverage guarantee is \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}.  The zero-threshold regime makes the gap explicit: there the target is an ordinary bounded mean with frontier \(n^{-1/2}\), while this radius continues to solve a local-regression problem whose natural scale is \(h_n^\beta\).  The question below is therefore posed for a component-level modulus; extending it to a modulus for the complete clamp functional, with the regular components, atom weights, their uncertainty, stratum aggregation, and a singular-design fallback included, is the substantive part of the problem and is open. The integrated radius averages the design-dependent affine optimization under the observed-design product law for the third split block and is defined stratum by stratum, replacing the analytic threshold-regression radius by a realized optimization problem.

\paragraph{Limitations.}
The scope of the results is the fixed finite-stratum design described in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, with outcomes bounded in \([0,1]\), known smoothness radius and exponent in the Hölder model, and a known polynomial-thinning exponent governing lower-tail overlap. The causal interpretation is attached to the declared full-data classes in \cref{obj:def:full-data-model-class,obj:def:continuity-full-data-model-class}, and the continuity-only conclusions are attached to the continuity-only observed and full-data classes in \cref{obj:def:continuity-model-class,obj:def:continuity-full-data-model-class}. Within that scope, the theorems characterize the minimax risk and honest expected-length orders for the lower-threshold clamp target and its causal lift. A further limitation concerns evidence rather than scope. The case made here for the stabilized estimator and the bias-aware interval is an asymptotic-order case, supported at the level of a single analytic calibration cell in \cref{obj:prop:one-cell-calibration}. Practical questions about total-Gram stabilization on singular realized designs and interval behavior at moderate sample sizes concern quantities whose finite-sample behavior need not resemble the eventual regime: the frequency of the Gram fallback, the realized coverage, and the realized expected length all depend on constants this analysis does not track. A systematic finite-sample evaluation across the regular, critical, atom-dominated, fixed-threshold, and near-zero regimes, reporting those quantities and comparing against unstabilized local polynomial and regular modified-policy benchmarks where the targets and conditions permit a fair comparison, is left to future work and is not attempted here.

\paragraph{Open questions.}
Two questions remain open.  The first is adaptation: the frontier established here is the known-class benchmark, with \(\beta\), \(L\), and \(\kappa\) fixed and available to the procedure, and whether the same frontier is attainable when those constants are unknown, and at what logarithmic cost, is not settled by the present analysis.  The second concerns exact constants rather than rates. Modulus-based confidence-interval theory describes the difficulty of such problems through the local modulus of a functional over a smoothness class; \citet{Cai2004} gives a general adaptation framework for confidence intervals, while \citet{ArmstrongKolesar2018OptimalInference,ArmstrongKolesar2020} develop honest inference procedures that use modulus calculations for bias-aware intervals. The honest expected-length lower bound proved here is obtained by direct two-point and localized-perturbation arguments, and it matches the risk frontier in order. The clamp target is linear in the outcome regression but includes the design-dependent weight \(q_x(\delta_n)\), so sharp modulus calibration is treated as a separate question. The exact-modulus constructions in \cref{obj:def:exact-modulus-handle,obj:synth_21,obj:synth_20} identify one concrete candidate for studying sharp honest length: use the total-Gram center, compute the realized affine-modulus radius on the third split block, and compare its worst integrated radius to the minimax expected-length benchmark.

\begin{remarkv}{oeq:sharp-constant}[Sharp length calibration]
The sharp constant question asks for the following property. For every
\[
 J\in\mathbb N,\qquad
 \beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\alpha\in\mathbb R
\]
satisfying
\[
 J>0,\quad \beta>0,\quad \kappa\geq0,\quad L>0,\quad c_->0,\quad
 c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+,\quad
 0<p_{\min}\leq \frac1J,\quad
 0<\bar\delta<1,\quad 0<\alpha<\frac12,
\]
there is a constant \(c>0\) such that every deterministic threshold sequence
\(\delta_{\bullet}=(\delta_n)_{n\geq1}\) with \(0\leq\delta_n\leq\bar\delta\) for all \(n\), and whose phase-boundary ratio satisfies either
\[
 \frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}\longrightarrow q
 \quad\text{for some }q\geq0
\]
or
\[
 \frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}\longrightarrow\infty,
\]
has the following simultaneous calibration properties. First,
\[
 \frac{L_n^\star}{r_n}\longrightarrow c,
 \qquad
 r_n=n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta,
\]
where \(h_n\) is the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} and \(r_n\) is the frontier from \cref{obj:def:frontier}. Second, there exists a deterministic sequence
\(B_{\bullet}=(B_n)_{n\geq1}\) of admissible three-way split blocks such that, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
 \inf_{P\in\mathcal M}
 P^{\otimes n}
 \left\{
   \theta_{\delta_n}(P)\in C^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}
 \right\}
 \geq 1-\alpha,
\]
where \(C^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}\) is the exact-modulus interval obtained from the split \(B_n\) using the exact-modulus construction of \cref{obj:def:exact-modulus-handle}. Its worst-case expected length satisfies
\[
 \frac{\ell^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}}{r_n}\longrightarrow c.
\]
Finally, the same sequence \(B_{\bullet}\) admits least-favorable mixed-experiment witnesses: there are a constant \(D>0\), an amplitude \(0<\mathsf{amplitude}\leq1/4\), and sequences
\[
 P_0,P_{\mathrm{reg}},P_{\mathrm{loc}}:\mathbb N\to\mathcal M
\]
such that, for all sufficiently large \(n\), the three laws satisfy the clamp-model member conditions; their \(n\)-sample laws are the product laws in \cref{obj:ass:iid-sampling}; the pair \((P_0(n),P_{\mathrm{reg}}(n))\) has the declared global Bernoulli shift at scale \(n^{-1/2}\); the pair \((P_0(n),P_{\mathrm{loc}}(n))\) has the declared localized Bernoulli perturbation at \(\beta\), \(\delta_n\), \(h_n\), and \(\mathsf{amplitude}\); and
\[
 \chi^2\!\left(P_{\mathrm{reg}}(n)^{\otimes n}\middle\Vert P_0(n)^{\otimes n}\right)\leq D,
 \qquad
 \chi^2\!\left(P_{\mathrm{loc}}(n)^{\otimes n}\middle\Vert P_0(n)^{\otimes n}\right)\leq D.
\]
With
\[
 \Delta_n^{\mathrm{mix}}
 =
 \left|\theta_{\delta_n}\!\left(P_{\mathrm{reg}}(n)\right)-
          \theta_{\delta_n}\!\left(P_0(n)\right)\right|
  +
 \left|\theta_{\delta_n}\!\left(P_{\mathrm{loc}}(n)\right)-
          \theta_{\delta_n}\!\left(P_0(n)\right)\right|,
\]
and with \(W_{n,B_n}\) denoting the worst integrated exact-modulus radius from
\cref{obj:def:exact-modulus-handle} computed using the third block in \(B_n\), the matching limits are
\[
 \frac{\Delta_n^{\mathrm{mix}}}{2W_{n,B_n}}\longrightarrow1,
 \qquad
 \frac{\ell^{\mathrm{mod}}_{n,B_n}}{2W_{n,B_n}}\longrightarrow1,
 \qquad
 \frac{2W_{n,B_n}}{L_n^\star}\longrightarrow1.
\]
\end{remarkv}

A positive answer to \cref{obj:oeq:sharp-constant} would identify the sharp asymptotic full-length constant for the exact-modulus interval across the phase regimes covered by the displayed threshold condition. It would also connect the mixed regular-and-local perturbation comparison directly to the integrated realized radius, sharpening the order-optimal honest interval of \cref{obj:thm:honest-length} into an exact asymptotic calibration.


\appendix

\section*{Appendices}

\section{Proofs and verification note}
\label{sec:appendix-proofs-verification}

This appendix collects the auxiliary statements that support the main risk, honest-length, causal-bridge, and continuity-only conclusions. The statements are organized by mathematical role: the clamp pushforward and support facts, the bandwidth and local-design algebra, the stabilized estimator and interval bounds, the phase calculations, the population moment identities underlying total-Gram stabilization, and the uniqueness statement for the continuity-only regression extension.

The first two propositions record the measure-theoretic behavior of the exact lower-threshold clamp. They connect the deterministic policy in \cref{obj:def:clamp-policy} to the mixed law that appears in the observed clamp functional in \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional}.

\begin{propositionv}{prop:pushforward-setup}[Clamp pushforward decomposition]
Let \(J\) be a positive integer, let \(P\in\mathcal M\) satisfy \cref{obj:def:model-class}, and let \(d_\delta(a)=\max\{a,\delta\}\) as in \cref{obj:def:clamp-policy}. Suppose the constants satisfy:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Smoothness and thinning.)} \(\beta>0\), \(\kappa\geq 0\), \(L>0\), and \(0<c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+\).
\item \textbf{(Stratum mass.)} \(0<p_{\min}\leq 1/J\).
\item \textbf{(Threshold and level.)} \(0<\bar\delta<1\) and \(0<\alpha<1/2\).
\item \textbf{(Evaluation point.)} \(x\in\mathcal X\) and \(0\leq\delta\leq\bar\delta\).
\end{itemize}
Define the lower-tail atom mass
\[
q_x(\delta)=\int_{[0,\delta]}\pi_x(a)\,da .
\]
Then the conditional pushforward law of the clamped treatment satisfies
\[
P\{d_\delta(A)\in da\mid X=x\}
=
P\{A\in da,\ A\in(\delta,1]\mid X=x\}
+
q_x(\delta)\,\delta_\delta(da),
\]
and the atom mass obeys
\[
\frac{c_-\,\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}
\leq
q_x(\delta)
\leq
\frac{c_+\,\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}.
\]
\end{propositionv}

\Cref{obj:prop:pushforward-setup} supplies the exact mixed-measure decomposition induced by the clamp. The continuous part retains the natural treatment values above the threshold, and the lower tail becomes a point mass at the threshold whose order is \(\delta^{\kappa+1}\) under \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}. This atom-size calculation is the source of the weighting in \cref{obj:def:frontier} and \cref{obj:def:continuity-frontier}.

\begin{propositionv}{prop:policy-support}[Policy support preservation]
Let \(P\), \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\alpha\), \(x\), and \(\delta\) satisfy:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Model.)} \(P\in\mathcal M\) satisfies \cref{obj:def:model-class} with constants \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min}\).
\item \textbf{(Regime constants.)} \(J\ge 1\), \(0<\beta\), \(0\le \kappa\), \(0<L\), \(0<c_-\le \kappa+1\le c_+\), \(0<p_{\min}\le 1/J\), \(0<\bar\delta<1\), and \(0<\alpha<1/2\).
\item \textbf{(Stratum and threshold.)} \(x\in\mathcal X\) and \(0\le \delta\le \bar\delta\).
\end{itemize}
Then, for conditional-treatment almost every \(a\) in stratum \(x\), the clamped value \(d_\delta(a)=\max\{a,\delta\}\) from \cref{obj:def:clamp-policy} is a support point of the conditional natural-treatment law given \(X=x\): every open neighborhood \(U\) of \(d_\delta(a)\) has positive conditional-treatment measure in stratum \(x\),
\[
0<\Pr_P(A\in U\mid X=x).
\]
\end{propositionv}

\Cref{obj:prop:policy-support} records the support property used by the causal bridge in \cref{obj:prop:causal-bridge}. The polynomial lower envelope gives positive conditional treatment measure in every neighborhood of the clamp value, so the structural response at the clamped treatment is evaluated on the support generated by the observed design.

\Cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance,obj:lem:local-gram-coercivity,obj:lem:good-gram-weights,obj:lem:weight-energy-balance} establish the bandwidth normalization and the realized local-polynomial algebra used by the total-Gram estimator. These statements translate the thinning envelope into local sample information and deterministic weight identities.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:shifted-moment-coercivity}[Uniform shifted coercivity]
Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Degree.)} Fix a polynomial degree \(\ell\in\mathbb N\).
\item \textbf{(Exponent.)} Fix a thinning exponent \(\kappa\in\mathbb R\).
\item \textbf{(Nonnegativity.)} The exponent satisfies \(0\le \kappa\), as in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}.
\item \textbf{(Reference matrix.)} Let \(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}\) denote the normalized population reference moment matrix in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}.
\end{itemize}
Then there exists a constant \(c=c(\ell,\kappa)>0\) such that, for every shift \(\rho\ge 0\) and every vector \(z=(z_0,\ldots,z_\ell)\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}\),
\[
c\sum_{j=0}^{\ell} z_j^2
\le
z^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}z .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:shifted-moment-coercivity}]
For \(z=(z_0,\ldots,z_\ell)\), set
\[
P_z(u)=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}z_i u^i
\]
and
\[
E_\kappa(z)
=
\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
\frac{z_i z_j}{i+j+\kappa+1}.
\]
Since \(i+j+\kappa>-1\), expansion of the square and the identity
\(\int_0^1 u^{i+j+\kappa}\,du=(i+j+\kappa+1)^{-1}\) give
\[
E_\kappa(z)
=
\int_0^1 P_z(u)^2u^\kappa\,du .
\] % lean: powerPolynomialEnergy_eq_integral

If \(z\ne0\), then \(P_z\) is a nonzero polynomial. Its zero set in \((0,1)\) is finite, so choose \(u_0\in(0,1)\) with \(P_z(u_0)\ne0\). At this point
\(P_z(u_0)^2u_0^\kappa>0\), while the integrand is continuous and nonnegative on \([0,1]\). Hence
\[
E_\kappa(z)>0
\qquad (z\ne0).
\] % lean: powerPolynomialEnergy_pos

The map \(z\mapsto E_\kappa(z)\) is continuous, because it is a finite sum of quadratic coordinate terms. Let
\[
S=\{z\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}:\|z\|_\infty=1\}.
\]
The set \(S\) is compact and nonempty, so \(E_\kappa\) attains its minimum on \(S\), say at \(z^\circ\in S\). Since \(z^\circ\ne0\), strict positivity of \(E_\kappa\) at nonzero vectors gives \(E_\kappa(z^\circ)>0\). Define
\[
c=\frac{E_\kappa(z^\circ)}{\ell+1}.
\]
Then \(c>0\). For \(z=0\), the desired bound against \(E_\kappa(z)\) is immediate. For \(z\ne0\), put
\[
r=\|z\|_\infty,
\qquad
v=r^{-1}z .
\]
Then \(v\in S\), \(E_\kappa(z)=r^2E_\kappa(v)\), and
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}z_j^2\le(\ell+1)r^2.
\]
Therefore
\[
c\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}z_j^2
\le
E_\kappa(z^\circ)r^2
\le
E_\kappa(v)r^2
=
E_\kappa(z).
\] % lean: powerPolynomialEnergy_coercive

It remains to compare this unshifted energy with the normalized shifted moment matrix. Fix \(\rho\ge0\), and define
\[
D_\rho=\int_0^1(\rho+u)^\kappa\,du,
\qquad
N_\rho(z)=\int_0^1P_z(u)^2(\rho+u)^\kappa\,du .
\]
By the definition of \(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}\),
\[
z^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}z
=
\frac{N_\rho(z)}{D_\rho}.
\] % lean: matrixQuadratic_refMomentMatrix_eq

The denominator is positive. Indeed, \((\rho+u)^\kappa\) is continuous and nonnegative on \([0,1]\), and it is strictly positive near \(u=1\). With
\[
R_\rho=(\rho+1)^\kappa,
\]
monotonicity of \(t\mapsto t^\kappa\) on \([0,\infty)\) gives
\[
D_\rho\le R_\rho .
\] % lean: uniform_shifted_power_moment_coercivity

For \(u\in[0,1]\),
\[
u(\rho+1)\le \rho+u,
\]
and hence
\[
R_\rho u^\kappa
=
\{u(\rho+1)\}^\kappa
\le
(\rho+u)^\kappa .
\]
Multiplying by \(P_z(u)^2\) and integrating yields
\[
R_\rho E_\kappa(z)
\le
N_\rho(z).
\] % lean: uniform_shifted_power_moment_coercivity

Since \(E_\kappa(z)\ge0\), the inequalities \(D_\rho\le R_\rho\) and
\(R_\rho E_\kappa(z)\le N_\rho(z)\) imply
\[
E_\kappa(z)\le \frac{N_\rho(z)}{D_\rho}.
\] % lean: uniform_shifted_power_moment_coercivity

Combining the coercive bound for \(E_\kappa\) with the displayed expression for
\(z^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}z\) gives, for every \(\rho\ge0\),
\[
c\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}z_j^2
\le
z^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}z .
\] % lean: uniform_shifted_power_moment_coercivity
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance} turns the infimum definition of \(h_n\) into an eventual equality at the information boundary. This equality is the normalization used in the risk upper bound and in the expected weight-energy calculation.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:lambda-star-positive}[Positive Gram constant]
In the local-design notation of \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, suppose:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Degree.)} The polynomial degree is \(\ell\in\mathbb N\).
\item \textbf{(Thinning exponent.)} The exponent satisfies \(\kappa\ge 0\), as in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}.
\item \textbf{(Envelope constants.)} The envelope constants satisfy \(c_->0\) and \(c_+>0\).
\end{itemize}
Then the uniform population Gram lower-bound constant
\[
\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)
=
\frac{c_-}{c_+}\inf_{\rho\ge 0}\lambda_{\min}(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho})
\]
is strictly positive:
\[
\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)>0.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:lambda-star-positive}]
By \cref{obj:lem:shifted-moment-coercivity}, there is a constant
\(c=c(\ell,\kappa)>0\) such that, for every \(\rho\ge0\) and every
\(z\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}\),
\[
c\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}z_j^2
\le
z^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}z .
\] % lean: uniform_shifted_power_moment_coercivity

Taking the infimum over all unit vectors gives
\[
c\le \lambda_{\min}(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho})
\qquad\text{for every }\rho\ge0 .
\] % lean: lambdaStar_pos

The indexing set \(\{\rho:\rho\ge0\}\) is nonempty, so
\[
c
\le
\inf_{\rho\ge0}\lambda_{\min}(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}).
\] % lean: lambdaStar_pos

Because \(c_->0\) and \(c_+>0\),
\[
\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)
=
\frac{c_-}{c_+}\inf_{\rho\ge0}
\lambda_{\min}(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho})
\ge
\frac{c_-}{c_+}c
>
0 .
\] % lean: lambdaStar_pos
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:local-gram-coercivity} gives the population conditioning bound for the local monomial design. The lower bound is scaled by the local window mass, so it remains compatible with the global polynomial-thinning envelope in the lower-endpoint window.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:power-window-integral-lower}[Power window integral lower bound]
Using the thinning-exponent notation of \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}, let \(\delta\) be a threshold, \(h\) a window width, and \(\kappa\) an exponent. Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Threshold.)} \(0\le \delta\).
\item \textbf{(Window width.)} \(0<h\).
\item \textbf{(Exponent.)} \(0\le \kappa\).
\end{itemize}
Then
\[
\frac{h}{2}\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^{\kappa}
\le
\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h} a^{\kappa}\,da .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:power-window-integral-lower}]
Set
\[
m=\delta+\frac h2 .
\]
Then
\[
\delta\le m\le \delta+h,
\qquad
0\le m,
\qquad
\frac{\delta+h}{2}\le m .
\] % lean: integral_rpow_window_lower

For every \(a\in[m,\delta+h]\), the hypotheses \(0\le\delta\) and \(0<h\) give
\[
0\le \frac{\delta+h}{2},
\]
and the bound \((\delta+h)/2\le m\) recorded above, together with \(m\le a\), gives
\[
\frac{\delta+h}{2}\le m\le a .
\]
Together with \(\kappa\ge0\), monotonicity of real powers on nonnegative bases yields
\[
\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa
\le
a^\kappa .
\]
Thus
\[
\int_m^{\delta+h}
\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa\,da
\le
\int_m^{\delta+h} a^\kappa\,da .
\] % lean: integral_rpow_window_lower

The left-hand side is the constant integral over an interval of length \(h/2\):
\[
\int_m^{\delta+h}
\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa\,da
=
\frac h2\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa .
\] % lean: integral_rpow_window_lower

Since \(a^\kappa\ge0\) on \([\delta,\delta+h]\) and
\([m,\delta+h]\subseteq[\delta,\delta+h]\),
\[
\int_m^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da
\le
\int_\delta^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da .
\] % lean: integral_rpow_window_lower

Combining the window-length bound, the pointwise lower bound on \(a^\kappa\), and the
monotonicity of the window integral gives
\[
\frac h2\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa
\le
\int_\delta^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da .
\] % lean: integral_rpow_window_lower
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights} is the deterministic local-polynomial fact used on the good-design event. Moment reproduction gives the intercept interpretation at the threshold, while the absolute-weight and squared-weight bounds control the Hölder bias and stochastic radius in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:local-window-mass}[Local window mass identity]
Let \(P\), an observed-data law, belong to the model class \(\mathcal M\) of \cref{obj:def:model-class}, with conditional treatment densities, stratum masses, and polynomial thinning as in \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law,obj:ass:stratum-mass,obj:ass:polynomial-thinning} for constants \((\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min})\).  Fix:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Stratum.)} A stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\).
\item \textbf{(Threshold and width.)} A threshold \(\delta\in\mathbb R\) and a window width \(h\in\mathbb R\) with \(h>0\).
\item \textbf{(Window support.)} \([\delta,\delta+h]\subseteq[0,1]\).
\end{itemize}
Then the population mass of the local window in stratum \(x\) is
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{X=x,\ 0\le (A-\delta)/h\le 1\}\,dP
=
p_x\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}\pi_x(a)\,da.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:local-window-mass}]
Let
\[
E_x(\delta,h)
=
\{O=(X,A,Y):X=x,\ A\in[\delta,\delta+h]\}.
\]
Because \(h>0\), for every observation \(O\),
\[
0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
A\in[\delta,\delta+h].
\] % lean: scaledDose_mem_Icc_iff

Therefore
\[
\mathbf 1\left\{X=x,\ 0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}
=
\mathbf 1_{E_x(\delta,h)}(O).
\] % lean: localWindowWeight_integral_eq

Integrating this identity gives
\[
\int
\mathbf 1\left\{X=x,\ 0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}\,dP
=
P(E_x(\delta,h)).
\] % lean: localWindowWeight_integral_eq

The window-support assumption gives
\([\delta,\delta+h]\subseteq[0,1]\). Applying the conditional-density law in
stratum \(x\) to the Borel set \([\delta,\delta+h]\) yields
\[
P(E_x(\delta,h))
=
p_x\int_{[\delta,\delta+h]}\pi_x(a)\,da
=
p_x\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}\pi_x(a)\,da .
\] % lean: localWindowWeight_integral_eq

The asserted identity follows from the two displayed evaluations of the local window weight. % lean: localWindowWeight_integral_eq
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:weight-energy-balance} integrates the squared-weight scale over the random design. Under the balance equation, the average stochastic radius has the same \(h^\beta\) order as the deterministic local-polynomial bias term.

The following two lemmas are the finite-sample ingredients for the stabilized procedures. They combine bounded-outcome concentration, deterministic fallback branches, and the local-design controls above.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:local-gram-entry-integral}[Local Gram integral identity]
Fix a law \(P\in\mathcal M\) with declared constants \((\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min})\), as in \cref{obj:def:model-class,obj:ass:conditional-density-law,obj:ass:stratum-mass,obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}.  Let \(\ell\in\mathbb N\), \(x\in\mathcal X\), and \(\delta,h\in\mathbb R\).  Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Constant signs.)} \(\kappa\ge 0\), \(c_+\ge 0\), and \(p_{\min}>0\).
\item \textbf{(Window.)} \(h>0\) and \([\delta,\delta+h]\subseteq[0,1]\).
\item \textbf{(Entries.)} \(j,k\in\{0,\ldots,\ell\}\).
\end{itemize}
For \(u_\delta(a)=(a-\delta)/h\), write \(v_\ell(u)=(1,u,\ldots,u^\ell)\) as in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, and define
\[
\phi_j(O)=\mathbf 1\{0\le u_\delta(A)\le 1\}\,v_\ell(u_\delta(A))_j .
\]
Then
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{X=x,\ 0\le u_\delta(A)\le 1\}\,\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)\,dP(O)
=
p_x\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}
\pi_x(a)\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_j\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_k\,da .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:local-gram-entry-integral}]
Write
\[
T=[\delta,\delta+h],
\qquad
u_\delta(a)=\frac{a-\delta}{h}.
\]
Since \(h>0\), for every real \(a\),
\[
0\le u_\delta(a)\le1
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
a\in T .
\] % lean: scaledDose_mem_Icc_iff

Define the bounded measurable function
\[
f(a)
=
\mathbf 1\{0\le u_\delta(a)\le1\}\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_j\,v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_k .
\]
For \(u\in[0,1]\), the entries \(v_\ell(u)_j\) and \(v_\ell(u)_k\) are powers of
\(u\), so their absolute values are at most one. Hence
\[
|f(a)|\le1
\qquad\text{for every }a .
\] % lean: localWindowGramEntry_integral_eq

The signs \(\kappa\ge0\) and \(c_+\ge0\), together with the density envelope on
\([0,1]\), give the pointwise domination used for integrability: for
Lebesgue-a.e. \(a\in[0,1]\),
\[
0\le \pi_x(a)\le c_+a^\kappa\le c_+ .
\]
The last inequality follows from \(0\le a\le1\) and \(\kappa\ge0\), and it is
preserved after multiplication by \(c_+\ge0\). Hence
\[
\int_{[0,1]}\pi_x(a)\,da\le c_+<\infty .
\]
Consequently, if \(b\) is measurable and \(|b(a)|\le1\), then
\[
\int_{[0,1]} |b(a)|\pi_x(a)\,da
\le
\int_{[0,1]}\pi_x(a)\,da
<\infty,
\]
so bounded measurable multiples are integrable for the conditional treatment
measure in stratum \(x\). The hypothesis \(p_{\min}>0\) is carried with the
model hypotheses used below for the stratum identity. % lean: localWindowGramEntry_integral_eq

The stratum-restricted treatment law therefore has the following integral form:
for every bounded measurable \(g:[0,1]\to\mathbb R\),
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{X=x\}g(A)\,dP
=
p_x\int_{[0,1]}g(a)\pi_x(a)\,da .
\]
To see this, apply the conditional-density condition in
\cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law} first to indicators
\(g=\mathbf 1_B\), where \(B\subseteq[0,1]\) is Borel:
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{X=x\}\mathbf 1_B(A)\,dP
=
P(X=x,A\in B)
=
p_x\int_B\pi_x(a)\,da .
\]
Linearity gives the identity for nonnegative simple functions, monotone
convergence gives it for nonnegative measurable functions, and decomposition
into positive and negative parts gives it for bounded measurable \(g\); the
integrability just established makes both sides finite. % lean: stratumTreatment_integral_eq

Applying this stratum identity to the bounded measurable function \(f\) gives
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{X=x\}f(A)\,dP
=
p_x\int_{[0,1]}f(a)\pi_x(a)\,da .
\] % lean: conditionalTreatment_integral_eq_density

By the definition of \(\phi_j\) and \(\phi_k\),
\[
\mathbf 1\{X=x,\ 0\le u_\delta(A)\le1\}\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)
=
\mathbf 1\{X=x\}f(A).
\] % lean: localWindowGramEntry_integral_eq

Finally, since \(T\subseteq[0,1]\) and \(f(a)=0\) off \(T\), while
\(f(a)=v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_jv_\ell(u_\delta(a))_k\) on \(T\),
\[
\int_{[0,1]}f(a)\pi_x(a)\,da
=
\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}
\pi_x(a)\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_j\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_k\,da .
\] % lean: localWindowGramEntry_integral_eq

Combining the displayed stratum decomposition with the preceding indicator rewriting yields
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{X=x,\ 0\le u_\delta(A)\le 1\}\,\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)\,dP(O)
=
p_x\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}
\pi_x(a)\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_j\,
v_\ell(u_\delta(a))_k\,da .
\] % lean: localWindowGramEntry_integral_eq
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:stabilized-interval-honesty} gives the finite-sample coverage statement for the interval in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}. The interval combines split-block concentration with a bias-aware local radius; on singular local designs, the bounded fallback radius covers the whole possible threshold-mean contribution in the affected stratum.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:scaled-window-coercivity}[Scaled window coercivity]
Let \(\ell\in\mathbb N\), and let \(\lambda_\star=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)\) be the uniform population Gram lower-bound constant from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, formed with the lower and upper thinning constants in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}. Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Shape constants.)} \(\kappa\ge 0\), \(c_->0\), and \(c_+>0\).
\item \textbf{(Window.)} \(\delta\ge 0\) and \(h>0\).
\item \textbf{(Coefficient vector.)} \(v=(v_0,\ldots,v_\ell)\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}\).
\end{itemize}
Then
\[
\lambda_\star c_+
\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h} a^\kappa\,\mathrm da\right)
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell} v_j^2
\le
c_-
\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}
\left\{
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
v_j\left(\frac{a-\delta}{h}\right)^j
\right\}^2
a^\kappa\,\mathrm da .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:scaled-window-coercivity}]
Put
\[
\rho=\frac{\delta}{h},\qquad
D=\int_0^1(\rho+u)^\kappa\,du,\qquad
N=\int_0^1
\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j u^j\right)^2(\rho+u)^\kappa\,du .
\]
Since \(\delta\ge0\) and \(h>0\), one has \(\rho\ge0\).  The function
\(u\mapsto(\rho+u)^\kappa\) is continuous on \([0,1]\), is nonnegative there, and has value \((\rho+1)^\kappa>0\) at \(u=1\).  Hence it is positive on a neighborhood of \(1\), and the interval-integral positivity criterion gives \(D>0\).

By the definition of \(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}\) in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, expanding the quadratic form gives
\[
v^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}v=\frac{N}{D}.
\]
The uniform coercivity in \cref{obj:lem:shifted-moment-coercivity} supplies the lower boundedness used to compare the defining infimum in \(\lambda_\star\) with the \(\rho\)-term.  The usual rescaling from arbitrary vectors to the Euclidean unit sphere then gives
\[
\lambda_\star\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
\le
\frac{c_-}{c_+}\,
\frac{N}{D}.
\]
Indeed, for \(v=0\) this is immediate, while for \(v\ne0\) applying the unit-sphere minimum to
\(v/\{\sum_jv_j^2\}^{1/2}\) yields
\[
\inf_{\rho'\ge0}\lambda_{\min}(M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho'})
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
\le
v^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}v .
\]
Multiplying by the nonnegative factor \(c_-/c_+\) and using the displayed identity for \(v^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}v\) gives the bound
\(\lambda_\star\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2\le(c_-/c_+)\,v^\top M_{\ell,\kappa,\rho}v\).  Since \(c_+D>0\), multiplying by \(c_+D\) gives
\[
\lambda_\star c_+D\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
\le c_-N .
\]
% lean: lambdaStar_refMomentMatrix_coercive

It remains to transport this inequality from \([0,1]\) to the window.  With the change of variables
\(a=\delta+hu\),
\[
\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da
=
h\int_0^1(\delta+hu)^\kappa\,du
=
h\,h^\kappa
\int_0^1(\rho+u)^\kappa\,du
=
h\,h^\kappa D,
\]
because \(\delta+hu=h(\rho+u)\) and \(h>0\).  The same substitution gives
\[
\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}
\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j\left(\frac{a-\delta}{h}\right)^j\right)^2
a^\kappa\,da
=
h\,h^\kappa N .
\]
% lean: lambdaStar_scaled_power_window_coercive

Since \(h\,h^\kappa\ge0\), multiplying the unit-window inequality by this factor and using the two displayed identities gives exactly
\[
\lambda_\star c_+
\left(\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da\right)
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
\le
c_-
\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}
\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
v_j\left(\frac{a-\delta}{h}\right)^j\right)^2
a^\kappa\,da .
\]
% lean: lambdaStar_scaled_power_window_coercive
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:stabilized-risk-upper} supplies the estimator side of the upper bound in \cref{obj:thm:minimax-risk}. The retained-course and atom-mass empirical terms contribute the \(n^{-1/2}\) scale, while \cref{obj:lem:weight-energy-balance} aligns the threshold-regression stochastic scale with the Hölder bias scale after multiplication by the collapsed mass.

The phase analysis rests on a separate bandwidth comparison. The result below identifies the edge scale where the boundary form of the local design transitions to the interior form.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:intercept-weight-formula}[Intercept weight formula]
Fix the local-design notation of \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} and the split-block construction of \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}.  Let \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) be a deterministic three-way split with pairwise disjoint blocks, each of cardinality at least \(\lfloor n/4\rfloor\).  Fix a realized sample \(z=(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\), a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\), a degree \(\ell\), constants \(\kappa,c_-,c_+\), a threshold \(\delta\), a window width \(h\), and an index \(i\).

Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Good design.)} The good-design event \(\Omega_x\) holds for \(B_n,z,x,\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+,\delta,h\).
\item \textbf{(Regression block.)} The index satisfies \(i\in I_2\).
\item \textbf{(Stratum match.)} The realized stratum satisfies \(X_i=x\).
\item \textbf{(Local window.)} With \(U_i=(A_i-\delta)/h\), one has \(U_i\in[0,1]\).
\end{itemize}

Then the exact local-polynomial intercept weight satisfies
\[
w_{ix}=e_0^\top G_x^{-1}v_\ell(U_i).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-formula}]
Let
\[
K_i=\mathbf 1\{i\in I_2,\ X_i=x,\ 0\le U_i\le1\},
\qquad
U_i=\frac{A_i-\delta}{h}.
\]
On \(\Omega_x\), the totalized definition of the exact intercept weight uses the local-polynomial equivalent kernel:
\[
w_{ix}
=
\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}(G_x^{-1})_{0k}\,K_i\,U_i^k .
\]
% lean: interceptWeight_eq_equivKernel_of_good

The assumptions \(i\in I_2\), \(X_i=x\), and \(U_i\in[0,1]\) give \(K_i=1\).  Hence
\[
w_{ix}
=
\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}(G_x^{-1})_{0k}\,U_i^k
=
\left(G_x^{-1}v_\ell(U_i)\right)_0
=
e_0^\top G_x^{-1}v_\ell(U_i).
\]
% lean: interceptWeight_eq_mulVec
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-phases} is the algebraic input behind the phase diagram in \cref{obj:thm:phase-diagram}. It shows that the bandwidth is comparable to the boundary scale near the edge and to the usual thinned interior bandwidth when the threshold is asymptotically beyond that edge.

The total-Gram probability bound depends on population coercivity and elementary local-window identities. \Cref{obj:lem:shifted-moment-coercivity,obj:lem:lambda-star-positive,obj:lem:power-window-integral-lower,obj:lem:local-window-mass,obj:lem:local-gram-entry-integral,obj:lem:scaled-window-coercivity} isolate these deterministic and integral facts before they are assembled in \cref{obj:lem:total-gram}.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:good-gram-reproduction}[Good Gram reproduction]
Fix \(J,n,\ell\in\mathbb N\), a realized sample \(z=(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\), a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\), constants \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,\delta,h\in\mathbb R\), and deterministic split blocks \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) as in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}.  Use the local-design notation \(U_i\), \(N_x\), \(G_x\), \(\lambda_\star\), \(\Omega_x\), and \(w_{ix}\) from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}.

Assume that \(\lambda_\star>0\) and that the good-design event \(\Omega_x\) holds for \(B_n,z,x,\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+,\delta,h\).  Then the exact local-polynomial intercept weights reproduce every monomial coordinate at the threshold: for every \(j=0,\ldots,\ell\),
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2} w_{ix} U_i^j
=
\begin{cases}
1, & j=0,\\
0, & j\ne 0.
\end{cases}
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-reproduction}]
Write
\[
K_i=\mathbf 1\{i\in I_2,\ X_i=x,\ 0\le U_i\le1\},
\qquad
G_x=\sum_{i}K_i\,v_\ell(U_i)v_\ell(U_i)^\top ,
\]
where the sum over all sample indices is the same as the sum over \(I_2\), since \(K_i=0\) outside \(I_2\).  Set
\[
N=N_x,\qquad
a=\frac{\lambda_\star N}{2}.
\]
On \(\Omega_x\), \(N>0\), and the assumed \(\lambda_\star>0\) gives \(a>0\).  The good-design inequality is
\[
a\sum_{r=0}^{\ell}b_r^2
\le
b^\top G_x b
\qquad\text{for every }b\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}.
\]
If \(G_x b=0\), then the right side is zero, so \(\sum_rb_r^2=0\) and \(b=0\).  Thus \(G_x\) is invertible. % lean: coerciveMatrix_isUnit

On \(\Omega_x\), the weights are the equivalent-kernel weights,
\[
w_{ix}=K_i\,e_0^\top G_x^{-1}v_\ell(U_i).
\]
Consequently, for \(0\le j\le\ell\),
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}U_i^j
=
e_0^\top G_x^{-1}
\left\{\sum_iK_i\,v_\ell(U_i)U_i^j\right\}.
\]
% lean: interceptWeight_eq_equivKernel_of_good

Let \(e_j\) be the \(j\)-th coordinate vector.  Since \(v_\ell(U_i)^\top e_j=U_i^j\),
\[
\sum_iK_i\,v_\ell(U_i)U_i^j
=
\sum_iK_i\,v_\ell(U_i)v_\ell(U_i)^\top e_j
=
G_xe_j .
\]
Therefore
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}U_i^j
=
e_0^\top G_x^{-1}G_xe_j
=
e_0^\top e_j
=
\begin{cases}
1,&j=0,\\
0,&j\ne0.
\end{cases}
\]
% lean: goodGram_reproduction
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:shifted-moment-coercivity} gives a uniform lower eigenvalue bound for the shifted reference moment matrices. The bound is uniform in the shift parameter, which is what permits thresholds ranging from the boundary region to fixed positive interior points.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:intercept-weight-pointwise}[Pointwise intercept-weight bound]
Fix integers \(J,n,\ell\), a split \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\), a realized sample \(z=(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\), a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\), constants \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,\delta,h\in\mathbb R\), and an index \(i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\).  With \(N_x\), \(\lambda_\star\), \(\Omega_x\), and \(w_{ix}\) as in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Split.)} The split \(B_n\) has three pairwise disjoint deterministic blocks \(I_0,I_1,I_2\), each of cardinality at least \(\lfloor n/4\rfloor\), as in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}.
\item \textbf{(Positive population Gram constant.)} \(\lambda_\star>0\).
\item \textbf{(Good design.)} The good-design event \(\Omega_x\) holds at the realized sample \(z\).
\end{itemize}
Then the exact local-polynomial intercept weight at index \(i\) satisfies
\[
|w_{ix}|
\le
\left(\frac{\lambda_\star N_x}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{\ell+1}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-pointwise}]
Let
\[
N=N_x,\qquad
a=\frac{\lambda_\star N}{2}.
\]
On \(\Omega_x\), \(N>0\); together with \(\lambda_\star>0\), this gives \(a>0\).  The good-design inequality states that
\[
a\|b\|_2^2\le b^\top G_xb
\qquad\text{for every }b\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}.
\]
% lean: GoodGramEvent

First suppose that \(i\) is active, meaning \(i\in I_2\), \(X_i=x\), and \(U_i\in[0,1]\).  Put
\[
s=v_\ell(U_i),\qquad \xi_i=G_x^{-1}s\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}.
\]
The coercivity inequality implies the inverse bound
\[
\|G_x^{-1}s\|_2\le a^{-1}\|s\|_2 .
\]
Indeed, \(G_x\xi_i=s\), so
\[
a\|\xi_i\|_2^2\le \xi_i^\top G_x\xi_i=\xi_i^\top s\le \|\xi_i\|_2\|s\|_2,
\]
and division by \(\|\xi_i\|_2\) is harmless, with the case \(\|\xi_i\|_2=0\) immediate. % lean: coerciveMatrix_inv_mulVec_euclidean_le

By \cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-formula},
\[
|w_{ix}|
=
\left|(G_x^{-1}s)_0\right|
\le
\|G_x^{-1}s\|_2
\le
a^{-1}\|s\|_2 .
\]
Since \(U_i\in[0,1]\),
\[
\|s\|_2^2
=
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}U_i^{2j}
\le
\ell+1,
\]
and hence
\[
|w_{ix}|
\le
\left(\frac{\lambda_\star N_x}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{\ell+1}.
\]
% lean: goodGram_interceptWeight_pointwise

If \(i\) is inactive, then the local kernel factor in the weight is zero on \(\Omega_x\), so
\[
w_{ix}=0,
\]
and the same bound follows because the right side is nonnegative. % lean: interceptWeight_eq_zero_of_inactive
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:lambda-star-positive} verifies the positivity of the Gram constant used in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}. This makes the good-design event meaningful uniformly over the polynomial-thinning envelope.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:bandwidth-balance}[Eventual bandwidth balance]
Let \(h_n\) denote the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} formed with \(\delta_n,\beta,\kappa,\bar\delta\). Suppose that:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Hölder exponent.)} The exponent \(\beta\) appearing in \cref{obj:ass:holder-regression} satisfies \(\beta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Polynomial-thinning exponent.)} The exponent \(\kappa\) appearing in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning} satisfies \(\kappa\geq0\).
\item \textbf{(Threshold cap.)} \(0<\bar\delta<1\).
\item \textbf{(Threshold sequence.)} \((\delta_n)_{n\geq1}\) is a deterministic real sequence satisfying \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\) for every \(n\).
\end{itemize}
Then, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
0<h_n,\qquad
h_n\leq 1-\bar\delta,\qquad
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance}]
Put \(H=1-\bar\delta\) and \(p=2\beta+1\).  The hypotheses give \(H>0\) and \(p>0\).  Since \(H^pH^\kappa>0\), choose \(N\) so large that
\[
        N>\frac{1}{H^pH^\kappa}.
\]
Because \(1/(H^pH^\kappa)>0\), this choice gives \(N>0\), and hence every \(n\ge N\) satisfies \(n>0\).  For \(n\ge N\), define
\[
        F_n(h)=n h^p(\delta_n+h)^\kappa,\qquad 0\le h\le H .
\]
The threshold condition gives \(\delta_n\ge0\), so
\[
        F_n(H)
        = nH^p(\delta_n+H)^\kappa
        \ge nH^pH^\kappa
        >1,
\]
because \(H\le \delta_n+H\) and \(t\mapsto t^\kappa\) is nondecreasing on \([0,\infty)\).  For continuity on \([0,H]\), use the real-power convention \(0^0=1\).  If \(\kappa=0\), then \((\delta_n+h)^\kappa=1\) on the whole interval and \(F_n(h)=n h^p\), which is continuous.  If \(\kappa\ne0\), then \(\kappa>0\), and the map \(h\mapsto(\delta_n+h)^\kappa\) is continuous on \([0,H]\) because \(\delta_n+h\ge0\); multiplying by the continuous factor \(n h^p\) gives continuity of \(F_n\).  In both cases
\[
        F_n(0)=n\,0^p(\delta_n+0)^\kappa=0,
\]
since \(p>0\).  Continuity of \(F_n\) on \([0,H]\) therefore gives a point \(\eta_n\in[0,H]\) with
\[
        F_n(\eta_n)=1.
\]
This point is strictly positive, since \(F_n(0)=0\). % lean: infoBandwidth_eventually_balance

The same monotonicity gives strict increase of \(F_n\) on \([0,H]\): if \(0\le a<b\le H\), then \(a^p<b^p\), while
\[
        (\delta_n+a)^\kappa\le(\delta_n+b)^\kappa .
\]
Moreover \(n>0\) by the preceding choice of \(N\), and \(\delta_n+b>0\), so
\[
        n(\delta_n+b)^\kappa>0 .
\]
Multiplying the strict inequality for \(a^p\) and the weak inequality for the second power by this positive factor gives \(F_n(a)<F_n(b)\).  Consequently the crossing set in \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} is exactly
\[
        \{h\in(0,H]:F_n(h)\ge1\}=[\eta_n,H].
\]
Its infimum is \(\eta_n\), so \(h_n=\eta_n\).  Thus, for every \(n\ge N\),
\[
        0<h_n,\qquad h_n\le 1-\bar\delta,\qquad
        n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
% lean: infoBandwidth_eventually_balance
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:power-window-integral-lower} gives a simple lower bound on local design mass under the power envelope. It is used to compare the realized local count with \(h(\delta+h)^\kappa\).

\begin{lemmav}{lem:local-gram-coercivity}[Local Gram coercivity]
Fix an observed law \(P\in\mathcal M\) as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, with conditional-density, stratum-mass, and polynomial-thinning components as in \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law,obj:ass:stratum-mass,obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}.  Fix constants \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+\), and \(p_{\min}\), and a degree \(\ell\in\mathbb N\).  Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Design constants.)} \(\kappa\ge0\), \(c_->0\), \(c_+>0\), and \(p_{\min}>0\).
\item \textbf{(Local window.)} \(x\in\mathcal X\), \(\delta\ge0\), \(h>0\), and \(\delta+h\le1\).
\end{itemize}
For an observation \(O=(X,A,Y)\), set
\[
p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)
=
\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf 1\!\left\{X=x,\ 0\le \frac{A-\delta}{h}\le 1\right\}
\right],
\]
and, for \(j=0,\ldots,\ell\), define the local monomial feature
\[
\phi_j(O)
=
\mathbf 1\!\left\{0\le \frac{A-\delta}{h}\le 1\right\}
\left(\frac{A-\delta}{h}\right)^j .
\]
Then, with \(\lambda_\star=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)\) denoting the uniform population Gram lower-bound constant from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, every \(v=(v_0,\ldots,v_\ell)\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}\) satisfies
\[
\lambda_\star\,p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
\le
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}
v_jv_k\,
\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf 1\!\left\{X=x,\ 0\le \frac{A-\delta}{h}\le 1\right\}
\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)
\right].
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:local-gram-coercivity}]
Let
\[
        T=[\delta,\delta+h],
        \qquad
        q_v(a)=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j\left(\frac{a-\delta}{h}\right)^j,
        \qquad
        \phi_j(O)=\mathbf 1\!\left\{0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}
        \left(\frac{A-\delta}{h}\right)^j .
\]
The inequalities \(\delta\ge0\) and \(\delta+h\le1\) give \(T\subset[0,1]\).  Since \(h>0\), the condition \(0\le(A-\delta)/h\le1\) is equivalent to \(A\in T\).  Hence \cref{obj:lem:local-window-mass} gives
\[
 \mathbb E_P\!\left[
 \mathbf 1\!\left\{X=x,\ 0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}\right]
 =
 p_x\int_T \pi_x(a)\,\mathrm da .
\]
The left-hand side is \(p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)\).
% lean: localWindowWeight_integral_eq

For each pair \((j,k)\), \cref{obj:lem:local-gram-entry-integral} gives the corresponding density-weighted local Gram entry.  Summing those identities over \(j\) and \(k\), and expanding \(q_v(a)^2\), yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}
v_jv_k\,
\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf 1\!\left\{X=x,\ 0\le \frac{A-\delta}{h}\le 1\right\}
\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)\right]  \\
&\hspace{2em}=
p_x\int_T q_v(a)^2\pi_x(a)\,\mathrm da .
\end{aligned}
\]
% lean: localWindow_populationGram_coercive

The stratum-mass condition in \cref{obj:ass:stratum-mass} and \(p_{\min}>0\) give \(p_x\ge0\).  Combining the displayed local mass identity for \(p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)\) with the upper envelope in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning} gives
\[
        p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)
        \le
        p_x\,c_+\int_T a^\kappa\,\mathrm da .
\]
The lower envelope in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}, together with \(q_v(a)^2\ge0\), gives
\[
        p_x\,c_-\int_T q_v(a)^2a^\kappa\,\mathrm da
        \le
        p_x\int_T q_v(a)^2\pi_x(a)\,\mathrm da .
\]
% lean: localWindow_populationGram_coercive

By \cref{obj:lem:lambda-star-positive}, the assumptions \(\kappa\ge0\), \(c_->0\), and \(c_+>0\) imply \(\lambda_\star>0\); in particular \(\lambda_\star\ge0\).
% lean: lambdaStar_pos

Applying \cref{obj:lem:scaled-window-coercivity} with this \(\delta,h\) and coefficient vector \(v\) gives
\[
 \lambda_\star c_+\!\left(\int_T a^\kappa\,\mathrm da\right)
 \sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
 \le
 c_-\int_T q_v(a)^2a^\kappa\,\mathrm da .
\]
% lean: lambdaStar_scaled_power_window_coercive

Combining the mass upper bound with \(\lambda_\star\ge0\) and \(\sum_j v_j^2\ge0\), then applying the scaled-window coercivity bound and the lower density-envelope bound, gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\lambda_\star\,p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
&\le
p_x\,\lambda_\star c_+\!\left(\int_T a^\kappa\,\mathrm da\right)
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2 \\
&\le
p_x\,c_-\int_T q_v(a)^2a^\kappa\,\mathrm da \\
&\le
p_x\int_T q_v(a)^2\pi_x(a)\,\mathrm da .
\end{aligned}
\]
Using the Gram identity above for the final term proves
\[
\lambda_\star\,p_x^{\mathrm{loc}}(\delta,h)\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}v_j^2
\le
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}
v_jv_k\,
\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf 1\!\left\{X=x,\ 0\le \frac{A-\delta}{h}\le 1\right\}
\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)\right].
\]
% lean: localWindow_populationGram_coercive
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:local-window-mass} expresses the local-window probability through the stratum mass and conditional density. It is the population counterpart of the count \(N_x\) in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:good-gram-weights}[Good Gram weights]
Fix \(J,n,\ell\in\mathbb N\), a split \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) with pairwise disjoint blocks satisfying \(|I_r|\ge \lfloor n/4\rfloor\) for \(r=0,1,2\), an observed sample \(z=(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\), and a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\).  Let \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,\delta,h\in\mathbb R\).  Suppose:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive bandwidth.)} \(h>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive population Gram constant.)} The constant \(\lambda_\star\) from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} satisfies \(\lambda_\star>0\).
\item \textbf{(Good local design.)} The good-design event \(\Omega_x\) from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} holds for \(B_n,z,x,\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+,\delta,h\).
\end{itemize}
Then the exact local-polynomial intercept weights \(w_{ix}\) and rescaled coordinates \(U_i\) from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} satisfy, for every \(j=0,\ldots,\ell\),
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2} w_{ix} U_i^j=\mathbf 1\{j=0\}.
\]
They also obey the two deterministic weight bounds
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}|w_{ix}|
\le
\frac{2\sqrt{\ell+1}}{\lambda_\star},
\]
and, with \(N_x\) denoting the local count in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation},
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}^2
\le
\frac{4(\ell+1)}{\lambda_\star^2 N_x}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights}]
Write
\[
        \lambda=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+),
        \qquad
        N=N_x,
        \qquad
        G=G_x .
\]
On \(\Omega_x\), \(\lambda>0\), \(N>0\), and
\[
        v^\top Gv\ge \frac{\lambda N}{2}\sum_{r=0}^{\ell}v_r^2
        \qquad\text{for every }v\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}.
\]
Thus \(G\) is nonsingular: if \(Gv=0\), the coercivity bound \(v^\top Gv\ge(\lambda N/2)\sum_r v_r^2\) with \(\lambda N>0\) forces \(\sum_r v_r^2=0\), hence \(v=0\).  The resulting monomial identity is the good-Gram reproduction statement in \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-reproduction}. % lean: coerciveMatrix_isUnit

For active observations \(i\in I_2\) with \(X_i=x\) and \(0\le U_i\le1\), the weight is
\[
        w_{ix}=e_0^\top G^{-1}v_\ell(U_i),
\]
and all inactive terms in the sum over \(I_2\) have weight \(0\).  Therefore, for \(j=0,\ldots,\ell\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i\in I_2} w_{ix}U_i^j
&=
e_0^\top G^{-1}
\sum_{i\in I_2}
\mathbf 1\{X_i=x,\ 0\le U_i\le1\}v_\ell(U_i)U_i^j  \\
&=
e_0^\top G^{-1}G e_j
=
\mathbf 1\{j=0\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
% lean: goodGram_reproduction

Next let \(a=\lambda N/2\).  The pointwise conclusion to be used is \cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-pointwise}.  For an active \(i\), set \(s_i=v_\ell(U_i)\) and \(z_i=G^{-1}s_i\).  Coercivity and Cauchy--Schwarz give
\[
        a\|z_i\|_2^2
        \le z_i^\top Gz_i
        = z_i^\top s_i
        \le \|z_i\|_2\|s_i\|_2,
\]
hence
\[
        \|G^{-1}s_i\|_2\le a^{-1}\|s_i\|_2 .
\]
Since \(0\le U_i\le1\),
\[
        \|s_i\|_2^2=\sum_{r=0}^{\ell}U_i^{2r}\le \ell+1,
\]
and therefore
\[
        |w_{ix}|
        \le
        \left(\frac{\lambda N}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{\ell+1}
\]
for every active \(i\), while inactive weights vanish. % lean: goodGram_interceptWeight_pointwise

The number of active indices is \(N\).  Summing the pointwise bound gives
\[
        \sum_{i\in I_2}|w_{ix}|
        \le
        N\left(\frac{\lambda N}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{\ell+1}
        =
        \frac{2\sqrt{\ell+1}}{\lambda}.
\]
Squaring the same pointwise bound and summing gives
\[
        \sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}^2
        \le
        N\left(\frac{\lambda N}{2}\right)^{-2}(\ell+1)
        =
        \frac{4(\ell+1)}{\lambda^2N}.
\]
% lean: goodGram_weight_controls
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:local-gram-entry-integral} identifies each population Gram entry with a one-dimensional integral over the local treatment window. Combined with the density envelope, it connects the empirical Gram matrix in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} to the shifted reference matrices.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:weight-energy-balance}[Balanced weight energy]
Let \(J,n,\ell\in\mathbb N\), let \(P\) be an observed-data law on \(J\) strata, let \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) be a deterministic split, and fix a stratum \(x\in\mathcal X\).  Let \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\delta,h\in\mathbb R\), and define the observed product sampling law by
\[
P^{\otimes n}=\bigotimes_{j=1}^n P .
\]
Suppose that:
\begin{itemize}
