\item \textbf{(Model and sampling.)} \(P\) belongs to the observed model class \(\mathcal M\) in \cref{obj:def:model-class} with fixed constants \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min}\), including the polynomial-thinning, stratum-mass, and Hölder-regression components in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning,obj:ass:stratum-mass,obj:ass:holder-regression}; the observed sample is independently drawn from \(P\) as in \cref{obj:ass:iid-sampling}; and \(B_n\) is a three-way split as in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}.
\item \textbf{(Numerical conditions.)} \(n\ge 8\), \(\beta>0\), \(\kappa\ge0\), \(c_->0\), \(c_+\ge0\), \(p_{\min}>0\), and \(\delta\ge0\).
\item \textbf{(Bandwidth and design.)} \(h>0\), \(\delta+h\le1\), and the uniform population Gram lower-bound constant \(\lambda_\star=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)\) from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} satisfies \(\lambda_\star>0\).
\item \textbf{(Balance.)} The bandwidth is information-balanced:
\[
n h^{2\beta+1}(\delta+h)^\kappa=1.
\]
\end{itemize}
For the exact local-polynomial intercept weights \(w_{ix}\) from \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation},
\[
\int
\left(\sum_{i\in I_2} w_{ix}(z)^2\right)^{1/2}
\,dP^{\otimes n}(z)
\le
\left(
\frac{336\{4(\ell+1)/\lambda_\star^2\}}
{p_{\min}c_-/(2\cdot 2^\kappa)}
\right)^{1/2}
h^\beta .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:weight-energy-balance}]
Set
\[
        p=\mathbb E_P\!\left[
        \mathbf 1\!\left\{X=x,\ 0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}\right],
        \qquad
        D=\frac{4(\ell+1)}{\lambda_\star^2},
        \qquad
        K=\frac{p_{\min}c_-}{2\cdot 2^\kappa}.
\]
Because \(h>0\), the event in the definition of \(p\) is
\(\{X=x,\ A\in[\delta,\delta+h]\}\).  Since
\(\delta\ge0\) and \(\delta+h\le1\), this window is contained in
\([0,1]\).  The conditional-density, stratum-mass, and polynomial-thinning
components of \cref{obj:def:model-class}, equivalently
\cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law,obj:ass:stratum-mass,obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}, give
\[
\begin{aligned}
        p
        &=p_x\int_{[\delta,\delta+h]}\pi_x(a)\,da \\
        &\ge p_{\min}c_-\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da .
\end{aligned}
\]
For \(a\in[\delta+h/2,\delta+h]\), the assumptions
\(\delta\ge0\) and \(\kappa\ge0\) imply
\(a^\kappa\ge((\delta+h)/2)^\kappa\).  Hence
\[
        \int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da
        \ge
        \int_{\delta+h/2}^{\delta+h}
        \left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa da
        =
        \left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa\frac h2 .
\]
Thus
\[
        p_{\min}c_-\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa\frac{h}{2}
        \le p .
\]
Since \(\delta+h>0\),
\[
        \left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa
        =
        \frac{(\delta+h)^\kappa}{2^\kappa},
\]
and therefore
\[
        K\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa\le p .
\]
The constants satisfy \(K>0\), \(h>0\), and \((\delta+h)^\kappa>0\), so \(p>0\).
% lean: localWindowWeight_integral_lower

Let \(m=|I_2|\).  The admissible split-size condition in \cref{obj:synth_8}
gives \(|I_2|\ge\lfloor n/4\rfloor\), and together with \(n\ge8\) this yields
\[
        \frac n8\le m .
\]
Thus \(m>0\), and \(p>0\) gives \(mp>0\).  Let \(N_x(z)\)
be the local count in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, evaluated for the
displayed \((\delta,h)\).  The count decomposition used for the weight energy is
\[
 \sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}(z)^2
 \le
 \frac{2D}{mp}
 +D\,\mathbf 1\!\left\{N_x(z)\le\frac{mp}{2}\right\}.
\]
Indeed, on \(\Omega_x\), \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights} gives
\[
        \sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}(z)^2\le \frac{D}{N_x(z)};
\]
on \(\Omega_x^c\), the weights in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} are zero.
Consequently the energy is at most \(D\) on the low-count event
\(N_x(z)\le mp/2\), while on its complement the good-design case gives
\(D/N_x(z)\le2D/(mp)\) and the \(\Omega_x^c\) case gives zero.
Under \cref{obj:synth_16,obj:ass:iid-sampling}, the variables defining
\(N_x\) on the third split block are independent Bernoulli variables with mean
\(p\).  The Bernoulli lower-tail bound gives
\[
 P^{\otimes n}\!\left\{z:N_x(z)\le\frac{mp}{2}\right\}
 \le
 2\exp\!\left(-\frac{mp}{20}\right).
\]
Integrating the pointwise decomposition gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \int\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}(z)^2\,dP^{\otimes n}(z)
 &\le
 \frac{2D}{mp}
 +D\,P^{\otimes n}\!\left\{z:N_x(z)\le\frac{mp}{2}\right\} \\
 &\le
 \frac{2D}{mp}
 +2D\exp\!\left(-\frac{mp}{20}\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Jensen's inequality for the square root therefore gives
\[
 \int\left(\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}(z)^2\right)^{1/2}dP^{\otimes n}(z)
 \le
 \left\{
 \frac{2D}{mp}
 +2D\exp\!\left(-\frac{mp}{20}\right)
 \right\}^{1/2}.
\]
% lean: integral_sqrt_interceptWeight_energy_le

The balance condition rewrites the effective sample size as
\[
        n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa=h^{-2\beta}.
\]
Together with \(m\ge n/8\) and \(p\ge K h(\delta+h)^\kappa\), this gives
\[
        mp\ge \frac K8 h^{-2\beta},
        \qquad
        (mp)^{-1}\le \frac8K h^{2\beta}.
\]
Also \(\exp(-t/20)\le20/t\) for \(t>0\), applied with \(t=mp\).  Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \frac{2D}{mp}+2D\exp\!\left(-\frac{mp}{20}\right)
 &\le
 42D(mp)^{-1}  \\
 &\le
 \frac{336D}{K}h^{2\beta}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Taking square roots and using \(h>0\) yields
\[
 \left\{
 \frac{2D}{mp}
 +2D\exp\!\left(-\frac{mp}{20}\right)
 \right\}^{1/2}
 \le
 \left(\frac{336D}{K}\right)^{1/2}h^\beta .
\]
% lean: balancedEffectiveSample_sqrt_le

Substituting the definitions of \(D\) and \(K\) gives exactly
\[
\int
\left(\sum_{i\in I_2} w_{ix}(z)^2\right)^{1/2}
\,dP^{\otimes n}(z)
\le
\left(
\frac{336\{4(\ell+1)/\lambda_\star^2\}}
{p_{\min}c_-/(2\cdot 2^\kappa)}
\right)^{1/2}
h^\beta .
\]
% lean: integral_sqrt_interceptWeight_energy_balanced_le
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:scaled-window-coercivity} converts the normalized shifted-moment lower bound into the physical treatment scale. This is the population inequality used before applying concentration to the realized Gram matrix.

\Cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-formula,obj:lem:good-gram-reproduction,obj:lem:intercept-weight-pointwise} record pointwise properties of the exact intercept weights on the good-design event. They are deterministic consequences of the total Gram matrix and the affine reproduction equations.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:stabilized-interval-honesty}[Stabilized interval honesty]
Fix integers \(J\) and \(n\), deterministic split blocks \(B=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\), constants \(\beta,\kappa,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta,\alpha\), and a threshold \(\delta\).  Let \(h_n\) be the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} evaluated at \((n,\delta,\beta,\kappa,\bar\delta)\), and let \(\mathrm{CI}_n\) be the stabilized interval from \cref{obj:def:honest-interval} formed with split \(B\), bandwidth \(h_n\), and threshold \(\delta\).  Suppose that:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Split blocks.)} \(B=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\) consists of three deterministic, pairwise-disjoint subsets of \(\{1,\ldots,n\}\) with
\[
|I_0|\ge \lfloor n/4\rfloor,\qquad
|I_1|\ge \lfloor n/4\rfloor,\qquad
|I_2|\ge \lfloor n/4\rfloor .
\]
\item \textbf{(Regime constants.)} The declared constants satisfy
\[
J\ge 1,\quad
\beta>0,\quad
\kappa\ge 0,\quad
L>0,\quad
0<c_-\le \kappa+1\le c_+,\quad
0<p_{\min}\le 1/J,\quad
0<\bar\delta<1,\quad
0<\alpha<1/2 .
\]
\item \textbf{(Sample size.)} \(n\ge 4\).
\item \textbf{(Threshold range.)} \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\).
\item \textbf{(Interior bandwidth.)} \(0<h_n\) and \(\delta+h_n\le 1\).
\end{itemize}
Then \(\mathrm{CI}_n\) is an observed-sample measurable interval, and for every observed-data law \(P\) in the clamp model \(\mathcal M\) of \cref{obj:def:model-class},
\[
1-\alpha
\le
P^{\otimes n}\!\left\{
\theta_\delta(P)\in \mathrm{CI}_n(O_1,\ldots,O_n)
\right\},
\]
where \(\theta_\delta(P)\) is the unsmoothed observed-data clamp target from \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional}.
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:stabilized-interval-honesty}]
Write \(h=h_n\), and let
\[
        t_\alpha=\sqrt{\frac{\log(12J/\alpha)}{2}},
        \qquad
        b_0=\frac{t_\alpha}{\sqrt{|I_0|}},
        \qquad
        b_1=\frac{t_\alpha}{\sqrt{|I_1|}} .
\]
The regime assumptions imply \(t_\alpha>0\), and the split-size and sample-size assumptions imply \(|I_0|,|I_1|>0\).  The center \(\widehat\theta_n\) in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator} and every stratum contribution \(D_x\) in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval} are measurable functions of the observed sample.  Hence the endpoint maps
\[
        z\mapsto
        \max\!\left\{0,\widehat\theta_n(z)-b_0-\sum_xD_x(z)\right\},
        \qquad
        z\mapsto
        \min\!\left\{1,\widehat\theta_n(z)+b_0+\sum_xD_x(z)\right\}
\]
are measurable.  The radius \(b_0+\sum_xD_x\) is nonnegative and \(\widehat\theta_n(z)\in[0,1]\), so these endpoints are ordered and define an observed-sample measurable interval. % lean: honestInterval_observedMeasurable

Fix \(P\in\mathcal M\).  Define
\[
 f_0(O)=Y\mathbf 1\{A>\delta\},
 \qquad
 f_x(O)=\mathbf 1\{X=x,\ A\le\delta\}.
\]
By \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional} and the conditional-density component of \cref{obj:def:model-class},
\[
        \mathbb E_P f_0(O)=\nu_\delta(P),
        \qquad
        \mathbb E_P f_x(O)=p_xq_x(\delta).
\]
Let
\[
\mathcal B_0=
\left\{
\left|
\widehat\nu_n-\mathbb E_P f_0(O)
\right|>b_0
\right\},
\]
\[
\mathcal B_{1x}=
\left\{
\left|
\widehat b_x-P(X=x,A\le\delta)
\right|>b_1
\right\},
\]
and
\[
\mathcal B_{2x}=
\left\{
\Omega_x,
\ t_\alpha\left(\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}^2\right)^{1/2}
\le
\left|
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}\{Y_i-\mu^P_{X_i}(A_i)\}
\right|
\right\}.
\]
Hoeffding's inequality on the first two split blocks gives
\[
        P^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B_0)
        \le \frac{\alpha}{6J},
        \qquad
        P^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B_{1x})
        \le \frac{\alpha}{6J}
        \quad\text{for every }x .
\]
The self-normalized weighted Hoeffding bound on the third block gives
\[
        P^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B_{2x})
        \le
        2e^{-2t_\alpha^2}
        \le
        \frac{\alpha}{6J}
        \quad\text{for every }x .
\]
% lean: honest_radius_tail_le
% lean: honest_weighted_tail_le
% lean: block_weighted_tail_on_good_gram

Let
\[
        \mathcal B
        =
        \mathcal B_0\cup
        \bigcup_x\mathcal B_{1x}\cup
        \bigcup_x\mathcal B_{2x}.
\]
Since \(J\ge1\),
\[
        P^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B)
        \le
        \frac{\alpha}{6J}
        +J\frac{\alpha}{6J}
        +J\frac{\alpha}{6J}
        \le \alpha .
\]
% lean: honestInterval_coverage_model

On \(\mathcal B^c\), the retained-course and atom-mass deviations satisfy
\[
        |\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)|\le b_0,
        \qquad
        |\widehat b_x-p_xq_x(\delta)|\le b_1
        \quad\text{for every }x .
\]
In the notation of \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, set
\[
        \ell=\ell(\beta),
        \qquad
        \lambda_\star=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+).
\]
The regime assumptions give \(\ell\in\mathbb N\), \(\kappa\ge0\), \(c_->0\), and \(c_+>0\), so \cref{obj:lem:lambda-star-positive} yields
\[
        \lambda_\star>0 .
\]
% lean: totalGram_lambdaStar_pos
For a stratum \(x\) with \(\Omega_x\), the hypotheses \(h>0\), \(\lambda_\star>0\), and \(\Omega_x\) are therefore in force, and \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights} gives the reproduction identities
\[
        \sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}U_i^j=\mathbf 1\{j=0\},
        \qquad j=0,\ldots,\ell .
\]
With
\[
        T_x(a)=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
        \frac{(\mu_x^P)^{(j)}(\delta)}{j!}(a-\delta)^j,
\]
these identities imply
\[
        \sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}T_x(A_i)=\mu_x^P(\delta).
\]
The inactive-window weights are zero, and on active summands \(X_i=x\) and \(A_i\in[\delta,\delta+h]\subset[0,1]\).  The Hölder condition in \cref{obj:def:model-class} therefore yields
\[
\left|
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}\mu_{X_i}^P(A_i)-\mu_x^P(\delta)
\right|
\le
Lh^\beta\sum_{i\in I_2}|w_{ix}|.
\]
Since \(\mu_x^P(\delta)\in[0,1]\), projection onto \([0,1]\) is contractive around this target, and the decomposition
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}Y_i-\mu_x^P(\delta)
=
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}\{Y_i-\mu_{X_i}^P(A_i)\}
+
\left(\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}\mu_{X_i}^P(A_i)-\mu_x^P(\delta)\right)
\]
gives
\[
\left|
\widetilde\mu_x-\mu_x^P(\delta)
\right|
\le
Lh^\beta\sum_{i\in I_2}|w_{ix}|
+
\left|
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}\{Y_i-\mu^P_{X_i}(A_i)\}
\right|.
\]
On \(\mathcal B^c\cap\Omega_x\), the last absolute value is at most
\(t_\alpha(\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}^2)^{1/2}\), so
\[
\left|
\widetilde\mu_x-\mu_x^P(\delta)
\right|
\le
B_x,
\]
where \(B_x\) is the local regression bias-plus-noise radius in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}. % lean: localRegression_good_error_le_empirical

The two displayed deviation bounds imply the deterministic error bound
\[
        \left|
        \widehat\theta_n-\theta_\delta(P)
        \right|
        \le
        b_0+\sum_xD_x .
\]
To see the algebra, set \(a_x=p_xq_x(\delta)\) and \(\widehat a_x=\widehat b_x\).  The atom deviation gives
\[
        a_x\le \widehat a_x+b_1
        \quad\text{and}\quad
        |\widehat a_x-a_x|\le b_1 .
\]
Using \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator,obj:def:clamp-functional} and contraction of the projection around the target \(\theta_\delta(P)\in[0,1]\),
\[
\left|
\widehat\theta_n-\theta_\delta(P)
\right|
\le
|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)|
+
\sum_x
\left|
\widehat a_x\widetilde\mu_x-a_x\mu_x^P(\delta)
\right|.
\]
On \(\Omega_x\), the product decomposition
\[
\widehat a_x\widetilde\mu_x-a_x\mu_x^P(\delta)
=
(\widehat a_x-a_x)\widetilde\mu_x
+
a_x\{\widetilde\mu_x-\mu_x^P(\delta)\}
\]
and the bounds \(0\le \widetilde\mu_x\le1\), \(a_x\le\widehat a_x+b_1\), and
\(|\widetilde\mu_x-\mu_x^P(\delta)|\le B_x\) give
\[
\left|
\widehat a_x\widetilde\mu_x-a_x\mu_x^P(\delta)
\right|
\le
b_1+(\widehat b_x+b_1)B_x
=D_x .
\]
On \(\Omega_x^c\), both \(\widetilde\mu_x\) and \(\mu_x^P(\delta)\) lie in \([0,1]\), so
\[
\left|
\widehat a_x\widetilde\mu_x-a_x\mu_x^P(\delta)
\right|
\le
\max\{\widehat a_x,a_x\}
\le
\widehat b_x+b_1
=D_x .
\]
Together with \(|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)|\le b_0\), these are exactly the two branches of the interval contribution in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}. % lean: totalGramEstimator_error_le_honestRadius

Because \(\theta_\delta(P)\in[0,1]\), the two displayed branches of the interval contribution place \(\theta_\delta(P)\) inside the clipped interval \(\mathrm{CI}_n(z)\) for every \(z\in\mathcal B^c\).  Hence
\[
 P^{\otimes n}\!\left\{
 \theta_\delta(P)\in \mathrm{CI}_n(O_1,\ldots,O_n)
 \right\}
 \ge
 P^{\otimes n}(\mathcal B^c)
 \ge
 1-\alpha .
\]
% lean: clampFunctional_mem_honestInterval_of_deviations
% lean: honestInterval_coverage_model

Since \(P\in\mathcal M\) was arbitrary, the same lower bound holds for every law in the stated model class, together with the measurability assertion proved above. % lean: stabilizedInterval_uniformCoverage
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-formula} gives the explicit intercept-weight representation for observations in the local stratum-window. It is the algebraic formula behind the threshold regression term in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:stabilized-risk-upper}[Stabilized risk upper bound]
Assume that the declared constants satisfy
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Strata.)} \(J\geq 1\).
\item \textbf{(Smoothness and thinning.)} \(\beta>0\) and \(\kappa\geq 0\).
\item \textbf{(Model radii.)} \(L>0\), \(c_->0\), \(c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+\), and \(p_{\min}>0\) with \(p_{\min}\leq 1/J\).
\item \textbf{(Threshold and calibration.)} \(0<\bar\delta<1\) and \(0<\alpha<1/2\).
\end{itemize}
Then there is a constant \(C_{\mathrm{up}}>0\), depending on these declared constants, such that the following holds.  For every deterministic threshold sequence \((\delta_n)_{n\geq 1}\) with \(0\leq \delta_n\leq \bar\delta\) for every \(n\), and for every deterministic split sequence \(B_{\bullet}=(B_n)_{n\geq 1}\) in which each \(B_n=(I_{0,n},I_{1,n},I_{2,n})\) consists of pairwise disjoint subsets of \(\{1,\ldots,n\}\) with
\[
|I_{0,n}|\geq \lfloor n/4\rfloor,\qquad
|I_{1,n}|\geq \lfloor n/4\rfloor,\qquad
|I_{2,n}|\geq \lfloor n/4\rfloor,
\]
there is \(N<\infty\) such that, for every \(n\geq N\),
\[
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}-\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\right|
\leq
C_{\mathrm{up}}\left(n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\right),
\]
where \(\mathcal M\) is the model class in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, \(\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\) is the clamp target in \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional}, \(\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}\) is the total-Gram estimator in \cref{obj:def:total-gram-estimator}, \(h_n\) is the information-balance bandwidth for \(\delta_n\) in \cref{obj:def:bandwidth}, and the rate is the frontier in \cref{obj:def:frontier}.
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:stabilized-risk-upper}]
Let \(\ell=\lceil\beta\rceil-1\).  From \cref{obj:lem:total-gram}, choose constants \(C_T,c_T>0\) such that the population Gram constant
\(\lambda_\star=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)\) is positive and the following bound holds uniformly over \(P\in\mathcal M\), sample sizes, admissible splits, thresholds \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\), and strata \(x\in\mathcal X\): if \(h\) is the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} evaluated at \((n,\delta,\beta,\kappa,\bar\delta)\), then
\[
P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)
\le
C_T\exp\{-c_T n h(\delta+h)^\kappa\}.
\]
% lean: total_gram_stabilization
Define
\[
K_{\mathrm{noise}}
=
\frac12
\left(
\frac{336\{4(\ell+1)/\lambda_\star^2\}}
{p_{\min}c_-/(2\cdot2^\kappa)}
\right)^{1/2},
\qquad
K_{\mathrm{bias}}=\frac{2\sqrt{\ell+1}}{\lambda_\star},
\]
\[
K_{\mathrm{loc}}
=
K_{\mathrm{noise}}+LK_{\mathrm{bias}}+\frac{C_T}{c_T},
\qquad
K_{\mathrm{atom}}=\frac{c_+}{\kappa+1},
\]
and
\[
C_{\mathrm{up}}
=
2(1+J)(1+K_{\mathrm{atom}}K_{\mathrm{loc}}).
\]
The displayed constants are finite and nonnegative, and \(C_{\mathrm{up}}>0\), by the regime assumptions and \(\lambda_\star>0\). % lean: stabilizedWorstRisk_eventually_le_frontier

Fix \((\delta_n)\) satisfying \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\) for every \(n\), and fix an admissible split sequence \(B_\bullet\).  By \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance}, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
0<h_n,
\qquad
h_n\le 1-\bar\delta,
\qquad
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
% lean: infoBandwidth_eventually_balance
After increasing \(N\), also \(n\ge8\).  Then \(\delta_n+h_n\le1\).  Put
\[
r_{0,n}=n^{-1/2},
\qquad
a_n=\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta .
\]
Since \(|I_{0,n}|,|I_{1,n}|\ge\lfloor n/4\rfloor\) and \(n\ge8\), we have
\[
n\le8|I_{0,n}|,
\qquad
n\le8|I_{1,n}|,
\]
and therefore
\[
|I_{0,n}|^{-1/2}\le3n^{-1/2},
\qquad
|I_{1,n}|^{-1/2}\le3n^{-1/2}.
\]
% lean: splitBlock_invSqrt_le_root

The balance identity gives
\[
n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=h_n^{-2\beta}.
\]
Thus, using \(h_n\le1\) and \(\beta>0\),
\[
C_T\exp\{-c_T n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\}
\le
\frac{C_T}{c_T}h_n^\beta .
\]
Indeed, with \(e=n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=h_n^{-2\beta}\), the elementary bound
\(\exp(-c_Te)\le(c_Te)^{-1}\) gives \((C_T/c_T)h_n^{2\beta}\), and
\(h_n^{2\beta}\le h_n^\beta\). % lean: balancedGramTail_le

For any \(P\in\mathcal M\), the projection \(\Pi_{[0,1]}\) is one-Lipschitz and the finite sum over strata gives the deterministic decomposition
\[
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}-\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\right|
\le
\mathbb E|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_{\delta_n}(P)|
+
\sum_{x\in\mathcal X}
\left\{
\mathbb E|\widehat b_x-p_xq_x(\delta_n)|
+
|p_xq_x(\delta_n)|
\mathbb E|\widetilde\mu_x-\mu_x^P(\delta_n)|
\right\},
\]
where all expectations on the right are under \(P^{\otimes n}\). % lean: totalGramEstimator_risk_decomposition
The retained-course and atom-mass block averages are averages of \([0,1]\)-valued variables, hence
\[
\mathbb E|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_{\delta_n}(P)|
\le
\frac12 |I_{0,n}|^{-1/2},
\qquad
\mathbb E|\widehat b_x-p_xq_x(\delta_n)|
\le
\frac12 |I_{1,n}|^{-1/2}.
\]
% lean: retainedEstimate_l1_le
% lean: atomEstimate_l1_le
By \cref{obj:prop:pushforward-setup} and \(0\le p_x\le1\),
\[
|p_xq_x(\delta_n)|
\le
\frac{c_+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}
=
K_{\mathrm{atom}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}.
\]
% lean: abs_atomCoefficient_le_envelope

It remains to bound the local regression term.  On \(\Omega_x\), polynomial reproduction and the deterministic weight bound in \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights} give the Hölder bias bound
\[
\left|
\sum_{i\in I_{2,n}}w_{ix}\mu_X^P(A_i)-\mu_x^P(\delta_n)
\right|
\le
LK_{\mathrm{bias}}h_n^\beta .
\]
% lean: localRegression_holderBias_le
The centered noise term satisfies
\[
\mathbb E
\left|
\sum_{i\in I_{2,n}}w_{ix}
\{Y_i-\mu_X^P(A_i)\}
\right|
\le
K_{\mathrm{noise}}h_n^\beta,
\]
because the conditional variance is bounded by \(\frac14\sum_i w_{ix}^2\) and
\cref{obj:lem:weight-energy-balance} controls the integrated square-root weight energy. % lean: localRegression_centered_balanced_l1_le
On \(\Omega_x^c\), both the fallback value and \(\mu_x^P(\delta_n)\) lie in \([0,1]\), so the exceptional contribution is at most \(P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)\).  Combining the good-event decomposition with the noise, bias, and tail bounds gives
\[
\mathbb E|\widetilde\mu_x-\mu_x^P(\delta_n)|
\le
K_{\mathrm{noise}}h_n^\beta+LK_{\mathrm{bias}}h_n^\beta
+C_T\exp\{-c_T n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\}
\le
K_{\mathrm{loc}}h_n^\beta .
\]
% lean: localRegression_risk_le_noise_bias_bad

Before the split-size and tail simplifications, the finite-sample bound is
\[
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}-\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\right|
\le
\frac12 |I_{0,n}|^{-1/2}
+
J\left\{
\frac12 |I_{1,n}|^{-1/2}
+
K_{\mathrm{atom}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}
\left(
K_{\mathrm{noise}}h_n^\beta+LK_{\mathrm{bias}}h_n^\beta
+C_T\exp\{-c_T n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\}
\right)
\right\}.
\]
% lean: totalGramEstimator_risk_le_explicit
Using the two split-block inequalities above and the local bound just obtained, uniformly in \(P\in\mathcal M\),
\[
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat\theta^{\mathrm{TG}}_{n,B_n}-\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\right|
\le
2r_{0,n}
+
J\{2r_{0,n}+K_{\mathrm{atom}}K_{\mathrm{loc}}a_n\}.
\]
% lean: stabilizedWorstRisk_eventually_le_frontier
The model class is nonempty.  Take the centered Bernoulli law with \(p_x=1/J\), conditional treatment density \(\pi_x(a)=(\kappa+1)a^\kappa\), and outcome distribution Bernoulli with mean \(1/2\) conditional on \((X,A)\); this law has regression \(\mu_x^P\equiv1/2\) and belongs to \(\mathcal M\) under the regime assumptions. % lean: minimaxCenter_mem_model
Hence the same upper bound applies to the supremum over \(P\in\mathcal M\).  Since \(r_{0,n},a_n\ge0\),
\[
2r_{0,n}
+
J\{2r_{0,n}+K_{\mathrm{atom}}K_{\mathrm{loc}}a_n\}
\le
2(1+J)(1+K_{\mathrm{atom}}K_{\mathrm{loc}})(r_{0,n}+a_n).
\]
% lean: stabilizedWorstRisk_eventually_le_frontier
Finally, \(r_{0,n}+a_n\) is exactly the frontier rate in \cref{obj:def:frontier}.  This proves the asserted bound with the displayed \(C_{\mathrm{up}}\). % lean: stabilizedWorstRisk_eventually_le_frontier
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:good-gram-reproduction} states the monomial reproduction property in the same notation used by the estimator. This property turns local-polynomial weighting into an estimate of the regression value at the threshold.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:bandwidth-phases}[Bandwidth phase bounds]
There exist constants \(c_{\mathrm{bal}},C_{\mathrm{bal}},c_{\mathrm{far}},C_{\mathrm{far}}\) with
\[
0<c_{\mathrm{bal}}\le C_{\mathrm{bal}},
\qquad
0<c_{\mathrm{far}}\le C_{\mathrm{far}},
\]
such that the following assertions hold.  Let
\[
\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}
=
n^{-1/(2\beta+\kappa+1)} .
\]
For every \(J\), every observed law \(P\), every \(L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\alpha\), and every deterministic sequence \((\delta_n)_{n\ge1}\), suppose that:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Regime constants.)} \(J\ge1\), \(\beta>0\), \(\kappa\ge0\), \(L>0\), \(c_->0\), \(c_-\le \kappa+1\le c_+\), \(p_{\min}>0\), \(p_{\min}\le 1/J\), \(0<\bar\delta<1\), and \(0<\alpha<1/2\).
\item \textbf{(Thinning.)} The law \(P\) satisfies the polynomial thinning condition in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning} with exponent \(\kappa\) and constants \(c_-,c_+\).
\item \textbf{(Thresholds.)} \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\) for every \(n\).
\end{itemize}
Let \(h_n\) be the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} for \((n,\delta_n,\beta,\kappa,\bar\delta)\).  Then, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
c_{\mathrm{bal}}
\le
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
\le
C_{\mathrm{bal}},
\]
and
\[
c_{\mathrm{bal}}h_n^{-2\beta}
\le
n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
\le
C_{\mathrm{bal}}h_n^{-2\beta}.
\]
If \(\delta_n/\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\to\infty\), then, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
c_{\mathrm{far}}\bigl(n\delta_n^\kappa\bigr)^{-1/(2\beta+1)}
\le
h_n
\le
C_{\mathrm{far}}\bigl(n\delta_n^\kappa\bigr)^{-1/(2\beta+1)} .
\]

Moreover, for every \(D>0\), there exist constants \(c_{\mathrm{near}},C_{\mathrm{near}}\), depending on \(D\), with
\[
0<c_{\mathrm{near}}\le C_{\mathrm{near}},
\]
such that, under the same regime-constant, thinning, and threshold conditions, if
\[
|\delta_n|\le D|\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}|
\]
for all sufficiently large \(n\), then, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
c_{\mathrm{near}}n^{-1/(2\beta+\kappa+1)}
\le
h_n
\le
C_{\mathrm{near}}n^{-1/(2\beta+\kappa+1)} .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-phases}]
Set
\[
p=2\beta+1,
\qquad
c_{\mathrm{bal}}=C_{\mathrm{bal}}=1,
\qquad
C_{\mathrm{far}}=1,
\]
and choose
\[
c_{\mathrm{far}}=\min\{1,2^{-\kappa/p}\}.
\]
At this initial choice of witnesses, the quotient \(-\kappa/p\) is interpreted by the total real-division convention: it equals \(0\) when \(p=0\) and agrees with ordinary division when \(p\ne0\). Thus \(c_{\mathrm{far}}\) is a real constant for every value of \(\beta\). Since the base \(2\) is positive, \(2^{-\kappa/p}>0\), and therefore
\[
0<c_{\mathrm{far}}\le1=C_{\mathrm{far}}.
\]
Together with \(c_{\mathrm{bal}}=C_{\mathrm{bal}}=1\), this gives
\[
0<c_{\mathrm{bal}}\le C_{\mathrm{bal}},
\qquad
0<c_{\mathrm{far}}\le C_{\mathrm{far}}.
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases

Fix \(J,P,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\alpha\) and a deterministic threshold sequence satisfying the hypotheses. The regime assumptions give \(\beta>0\), \(\kappa\ge0\), and \(0<\bar\delta<1\), hence \(p=2\beta+1>0\). Let \(h_n\) be the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth}; thus
\[
h_n=\inf\{h\in(0,1-\bar\delta]:
n h^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h)^\kappa\ge1\}
\]
when the displayed set is nonempty, with fallback value \(h_n=1-\bar\delta\) when that set is empty. By \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance}, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
0<h_n,
\qquad
h_n\le1-\bar\delta,
\qquad
n h_n^{p}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
% lean: infoBandwidth_eventually_balance
The first balance assertion follows immediately:
\[
1
\le
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
\le
1 .
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases
Since \(h_n>0\),
\[
h_n^{2\beta+1}=h_n^{2\beta}h_n,
\qquad
h_n^{-2\beta}=(h_n^{2\beta})^{-1}.
\]
Dividing the balance identity by \(h_n^{2\beta}\) gives
\[
n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=h_n^{-2\beta},
\]
and therefore
\[
h_n^{-2\beta}
\le
n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
\le
h_n^{-2\beta}.
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases

Now assume
\[
\frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}}\to\infty,
\qquad
\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}=n^{-1/(2\beta+\kappa+1)}=n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}.
\]
On a sufficiently large eventual set, the balance identity holds, \(n>0\), and the convergence gives \(1\le\delta_n/\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\). Since \(\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}>0\) on this set,
\[
\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\le\delta_n,
\qquad
\delta_n>0.
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases
For those \(n\), \(\delta_n^\kappa\le(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\), so the balance identity implies
\[
h_n^p(n\delta_n^\kappa)\le1.
\]
Taking \(p\)-th roots gives
\[
h_n\le (n\delta_n^\kappa)^{-1/p} .
\]
% lean: balance_root_le_interior_scale
The comparison \(\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\le\delta_n\) gives
\[
1=n\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}^{p+\kappa}
\le n\delta_n^{p+\kappa}
=\delta_n^p(n\delta_n^\kappa),
\]
and hence
\[
(n\delta_n^\kappa)^{-1/p}\le\delta_n .
\]
% lean: interior_scale_le_threshold_of_edge_le
Consequently \(h_n\le\delta_n\), and therefore
\[
\delta_n+h_n\le2\delta_n .
\]
Using the balance identity again,
\[
1
=
n h_n^p(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
\le
n h_n^p(2\delta_n)^\kappa
=
h_n^p\,2^\kappa(n\delta_n^\kappa).
\]
Thus
\[
2^{-\kappa/p}(n\delta_n^\kappa)^{-1/p}\le h_n .
\]
% lean: interior_scale_le_balance_root
Under \(p>0\) and \(\kappa\ge0\), \(2^{-\kappa/p}\le1\), so the chosen constant satisfies \(c_{\mathrm{far}}=2^{-\kappa/p}\). With \(C_{\mathrm{far}}=1\), the far-threshold comparison follows:
\[
c_{\mathrm{far}}(n\delta_n^\kappa)^{-1/(2\beta+1)}
\le
h_n
\le
C_{\mathrm{far}}(n\delta_n^\kappa)^{-1/(2\beta+1)} .
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases

Finally fix \(D>0\). Set
\[
C_{\mathrm{near}}=1,
\qquad
c_{\mathrm{near}}=\min\{1,(D+1)^{-\kappa/p}\},
\]
again using the same total real-division convention for the displayed quotient. Since \(D+1>0\), the positive-base property of real powers gives \((D+1)^{-\kappa/p}>0\), and therefore
\[
0<c_{\mathrm{near}}\le1=C_{\mathrm{near}}.
\]
Fix again \(J,P,L,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\alpha\) and a deterministic threshold sequence satisfying the hypotheses, and let \(h_n\) be the corresponding bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth}. The regime assumptions again give \(p>0\) and \(\kappa\ge0\). Suppose that eventually
\[
|\delta_n|\le D|\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}|.
\]
On the eventual set where this bound and \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance} both hold, \(n>0\), \(\delta_n\ge0\), and \(\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}>0\), so
\[
\delta_n\le D n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}.
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases
The balance identity and \(h_n^\kappa\le(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\) give
\[
n h_n^{p+\kappa}\le1,
\]
hence
\[
h_n\le n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}.
\]
% lean: balance_root_le_edge_scale
Together with the threshold bound, this gives
\[
\delta_n+h_n\le(D+1)n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}.
\]
Therefore
\[
1
=
n h_n^p(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
\le
n h_n^p (D+1)^\kappa\bigl(n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}\bigr)^\kappa .
\]
Since \(n\bigl(n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}\bigr)^{p+\kappa}=1\), the displayed inequality \(1\le n h_n^p (D+1)^\kappa (n^{-1/(p+\kappa)})^\kappa\) rearranges to
\[
(D+1)^{-\kappa/p}n^{-1/(p+\kappa)}\le h_n .
\]
% lean: edge_scale_le_balance_root
Under \(p>0\), \(\kappa\ge0\), and \(D>0\), \((D+1)^{-\kappa/p}\le1\), so the chosen constant satisfies \(c_{\mathrm{near}}=(D+1)^{-\kappa/p}\). Since \(p+\kappa=2\beta+\kappa+1\), we obtain, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
c_{\mathrm{near}}n^{-1/(2\beta+\kappa+1)}
\le
h_n
\le
C_{\mathrm{near}}n^{-1/(2\beta+\kappa+1)} .
\]
% lean: bandwidth_phases
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:intercept-weight-pointwise} gives the pointwise weight control implied by Gram coercivity. Together with \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights}, it bounds both individual leverage and aggregate variance.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:total-gram}[Total Gram stabilization]
Let \(J\) be a positive integer and let the declared constants satisfy
\[
\beta>0,\qquad \kappa\ge 0,\qquad L>0,\qquad
0<c_-\le \kappa+1\le c_+,\qquad
0<p_{\min}\le 1/J,\qquad
0<\bar\delta<1,\qquad 0<\alpha<1/2 .
\]
Then there are constants \(C,c>0\), depending only on these declared constants, such that the uniform population Gram lower-bound constant \(\lambda_\star\) in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}, evaluated at \((\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)\) with \(\ell=\lceil\beta\rceil-1\), satisfies \(\lambda_\star>0\), and the following hold.

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Model and sampling.)} \(P\in\mathcal M\) as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, and \(O_1,\ldots,O_n\) are sampled according to \cref{obj:ass:iid-sampling}.
\item \textbf{(Split and threshold.)} \(I_0,I_1,I_2\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}\) are deterministic, pairwise-disjoint split blocks with \(|I_b|\ge \lfloor n/4\rfloor\) for \(b=0,1,2\), and \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\).
\item \textbf{(Bandwidth and stratum.)} \(h_n\) is the information-balance bandwidth from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} at \((n,\delta,\beta,\kappa,\bar\delta)\), and \(x\in\mathcal X\).
\end{itemize}

For the good-design event \(\Omega_x\) in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation},
\[
P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)
\le
C\exp\{-c\,n\,h_n(\delta+h_n)^\kappa\}.
\]
On \(\Omega_x\), the exact local-polynomial intercept weights \(w_{ix}\) in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation} satisfy, for every \(j=0,\ldots,\ell\),
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2} w_{ix} U_i^j
=
\begin{cases}
1, & j=0,\\
0, & j\ge 1,
\end{cases}
\]
and also obey
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}|w_{ix}|\le C,
\qquad
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}^2\le \frac{C}{N_x}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:total-gram}]
Set
\[
\ell=\lceil\beta\rceil-1,\qquad
\lambda=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+),\qquad
d=\ell+1 .
\]
The regime assumptions give \(\kappa\ge0\), \(c_->0\), and \(c_+>0\), so \(\lambda>0\) by \cref{obj:lem:lambda-star-positive}. % lean: totalGram_lambdaStar_pos

Define
\[
\eta=
\min\left\{
\frac1{20},
\frac{\lambda^2}{16d(4d+\lambda)}
\right\},
\qquad
K=\frac{p_{\min}c_-}{2\cdot2^\kappa},
\qquad
c=\frac{\eta K}{8},
\]
and
\[
A_{\mathrm{tail}}=2(1+d^2),\qquad
C=
A_{\mathrm{tail}}e^{8c}+\frac{2\sqrt d}{\lambda}+\frac{4d}{\lambda^2}+1 .
\]
The constants \(\eta,K,c,A_{\mathrm{tail}},C\) are positive and, together with \(\lambda\), depend only on the displayed regime constants. % lean: total_gram_stabilization

Fix \(P,n,B_n,\delta,x\) as in the statement, and write
\[
h=h_n,
\qquad
q(O)=\mathbf 1\left\{X=x,\ 0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\},
\qquad
\phi_j(O)=
\mathbf 1\left\{0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}
\left(\frac{A-\delta}{h}\right)^j .
\]
The regime assumptions give \(\beta>0\), \(\kappa\ge0\), \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\), and \(0<\bar\delta<1\).  The construction of \(h\) in \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} gives \(0<h\le1-\bar\delta\).  Indeed, if the crossing set in \cref{obj:def:bandwidth} is empty, then \(h=1-\bar\delta\).  If it is nonempty and \(z\) is a member, then \(n>0\), \(0<z\le1-\bar\delta\), \(\delta+z\le1\), and
\[
1\le n z^{2\beta+1}(\delta+z)^\kappa\le n z^{2\beta+1},
\]
so \(n^{-1/(2\beta+1)}\le z\).  Hence the infimum is bounded below by this positive number and above by any member of the set, giving \(0<h\le1-\bar\delta\).  Since \(\delta\le\bar\delta\), this also gives \(\delta+h\le1\). % lean: infoBandwidth_pos_le

Let
\[
p=\mathbb E_P q(O)
=
P\left\{X=x,\ 0\le\frac{A-\delta}{h}\le1\right\}.
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:local-window-mass,obj:lem:power-window-integral-lower} and the thinning and stratum-mass assumptions,
\[
p
=
p_x\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}\pi_x(a)\,da
\ge
p_{\min}c_-\int_{\delta}^{\delta+h}a^\kappa\,da
\ge
p_{\min}c_-\frac h2\left(\frac{\delta+h}{2}\right)^\kappa
=
K\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa .
\]
% lean: localWindowWeight_integral_lower

For every \(b\in\mathbb R^{\ell+1}\), \cref{obj:lem:local-gram-coercivity} gives
\[
\lambda\,p\,\|b\|_2^2
\le
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}
b_jb_k\,\mathbb E_P\{q(O)\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)\}.
\]
Moreover \(0\le q\le1\) and \(|\phi_j|\le1\) for every \(j\). % lean: localWindow_populationGram_coercive

Let \(m=|I_2|\), and let \(i_1,\ldots,i_m\) be the elements of \(I_2\) in increasing order.  For a block vector \(\omega=(\omega_1,\ldots,\omega_m)\), define
\[
N_{\mathrm{blk}}(\omega)=\sum_{r=1}^m q(\omega_r),\qquad
M_{\mathrm{blk},jk}(\omega)=\sum_{r=1}^m q(\omega_r)\phi_j(\omega_r)\phi_k(\omega_r),
\]
and put
\[
M_{jk}=\mathbb E_P\{q(O)\phi_j(O)\phi_k(O)\}.
\]
The deterministic ordered-block map
\[
T_{I_2}(O_1,\ldots,O_n)=(O_{i_1},\ldots,O_{i_m})
\]
satisfies, for every measurable block event \(E\),
\[
P^{\otimes n}\{T_{I_2}(O_1,\ldots,O_n)\in E\}=P^{\otimes m}(E),
\]
because the selected coordinates are independent and each has law \(P\).  Under this map,
\[
N_{\mathrm{blk}}(T_{I_2}z)
=
\sum_{i\in I_2}q(O_i)=N_x,
\]
and, for every \(j,k\),
\[
M_{\mathrm{blk},jk}(T_{I_2}z)
=
\sum_{i\in I_2}q(O_i)\phi_j(O_i)\phi_k(O_i)
=(G_x)_{jk},
\]
where the last equality is the entrywise definition of the local Gram matrix in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}.

Define the block good event
\[
\Gamma_x=
\left\{
\omega:
N_{\mathrm{blk}}(\omega)>0
\ \text{and}\ 
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}
 b_jb_k M_{\mathrm{blk},jk}(\omega)
\ge
\frac{\lambda N_{\mathrm{blk}}(\omega)}{2}\|b\|_2^2
\ \text{for every } b\in\mathbb R^d
\right\}.
\]
The preceding identities give the exact event identity
\[
\Omega_x=\{z:T_{I_2}z\in\Gamma_x\}.
\]
Indeed, \(q\) is the indicator of the stratum-window event, \(\phi_j\) is its local monomial coordinate, and \(\lambda=\lambda_\star(\ell,\kappa,c_-,c_+)\), so \(N_{\mathrm{blk}}\) and \(M_{\mathrm{blk}}\) are exactly the count and quadratic form appearing in \cref{obj:def:local-design-notation}. % lean: localizedGramGood_iff_goodGramEvent

If \(n\ge8\), the split condition gives \(m\ge n/8\), hence \(m>0\).  The lower bound on \(p\) gives \(p>0\).  Applying the localized empirical Gram concentration bound to the \(m\) independent block coordinates, with the population coercivity above and with \(0\le q\le1\) and \(|\phi_j|\le1\), gives
\[
P^{\otimes m}(\Gamma_x^c)
\le
A_{\mathrm{tail}}\exp\{-mp\eta\}.
\]
% lean: Causalean.Stat.Concentration.localized_empiricalGram_coercive_of_pos
By the ordered-block product identity and \(\Omega_x=\{z:T_{I_2}z\in\Gamma_x\}\),
\[
P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)
\le
A_{\mathrm{tail}}\exp\{-mp\eta\}.
\]
Combining \(m\ge n/8\) with the lower bound on \(p\),
\[
c\,n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa
\le
m\,p\,\eta .
\]
Hence
\[
P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)
\le
A_{\mathrm{tail}}\exp\{-mp\eta\}
\le
A_{\mathrm{tail}}\exp\{-c\,n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa\}
\le
C\exp\{-c\,n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa\}.
\]
% lean: total_gram_stabilization

If \(n<8\), then \(h\le1\), \(\delta+h\le1\), and \(\kappa\ge0\) imply
\[
n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa\le8.
\]
Thus
\[
1\le
A_{\mathrm{tail}}e^{8c}\exp\{-c\,n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa\}
\le
C\exp\{-c\,n\,h(\delta+h)^\kappa\},
\]
and the same probability bound follows from \(P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)\le1\). % lean: total_gram_stabilization

On \(\Omega_x\), \cref{obj:lem:good-gram-weights} gives, for every \(0\le j\le\ell\),
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}U_i^j
=
\mathbf 1\{j=0\},
\]
which is the displayed reproduction identity.  The same result also gives
\[
\sum_{i\in I_2}|w_{ix}|
\le
\frac{2\sqrt d}{\lambda}
\le C,
\qquad
\sum_{i\in I_2}w_{ix}^2
\le
\frac{4d}{\lambda^2N_x}
\le
\frac{C}{N_x}.
\]
% lean: goodGram_weight_controls
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:total-gram} assembles the local-window identities, shifted coercivity, and concentration into the good-design probability bound used by the stabilized procedures. The exponential bound makes the singular-design fallback negligible on the scales used in \cref{obj:thm:minimax-risk,obj:thm:honest-length}, while the on-event identities preserve the usual local-polynomial intercept behavior.

The last auxiliary lemma for the appendix supports the continuity-only causal and observed targets. It establishes pointwise uniqueness of the continuous conditional-regression extension on the threshold envelope.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:continuity-regression-extension-unique}[Unique continuous regression extension]
Let \(J\) be a positive integer, let \(\kappa,c_-,c_+,p_{\min},\bar\delta\in\mathbb R\), and let \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) be a continuity-only observed clamp model with these constants, as in \cref{obj:def:continuity-model-class}. Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Design constants.)} The constants satisfy
\[
0\leq \kappa,\qquad
0<c_-,\qquad
c_-\leq \kappa+1\leq c_+,\qquad
0<p_{\min}\leq \frac1J,\qquad
0<\bar\delta<1 .
\]
\item \textbf{(Continuous versions.)} For \(r=1,2\), \(\mu_r:\mathcal X\times\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) satisfies that \(a\mapsto \mu_r(x,a)\) is continuous on \([0,\bar\delta]\) for every \(x\in\mathcal X\).
\item \textbf{(Regression version property.)} For \(r=1,2\),
\[
\mathbb E_P[Y\mid X,A]=\mu_r(X,A)
\quad\text{\(P\)-almost surely.}
\]
\end{itemize}
Then, for every \(x\in\mathcal X\),
\[
\mu_1(x,a)=\mu_2(x,a)
\qquad\text{for every }a\in[0,\bar\delta].
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:continuity-regression-extension-unique}]
Fix \(x\in\mathcal X\).  The two regression-version assumptions imply
\[
\mu_1(X,A)=\mu_2(X,A)
\quad P\text{-a.s.}
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
Define functions on the real line by
\[
f_r(a)=
\begin{cases}
\mu_r(x,a),&a\in[0,\bar\delta],\\
0,&a\notin[0,\bar\delta],
\end{cases}
\qquad r=1,2.
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
The continuity hypotheses make these functions measurable.  Set
\[
B=\{a\in\mathbb R:f_1(a)\neq f_2(a)\}.
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
Then \(B\) is measurable and
\[
B\subset[0,\bar\delta]\subset[0,1].
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
The almost-sure equality of the two versions gives
\[
P\{X=x,\ A\in B\}=0.
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
The conditional-density identity in \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law} applies to this measurable subset of \([0,1]\), so
\[
0=P\{X=x,\ A\in B\}
=
p_x\int_B\pi_x(a)\,da.
\]% lean: CondDensityLaw
The stratum-mass condition in \cref{obj:ass:stratum-mass}, together with \(p_{\min}>0\), gives \(p_x>0\).  Hence
\[
\int_B\pi_x(a)\,da=0.
\]% lean: StratumMass

It remains to justify the zero-integral argument on \(B\).  The conditional-density measurability in \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law} gives the needed measurability on the restricted set.  Since \(B\subset[0,1]\), the set \(B\) has finite Lebesgue measure.  For Lebesgue-a.e. \(a\in B\), the nonnegativity in \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law}, the upper envelope in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}, and the design inequalities \(0\leq\kappa\) and \(0\leq c_+\) give
\[
|\pi_x(a)|=\pi_x(a)
\leq c_+a^\kappa
\leq c_+,
\qquad 0\leq a\leq1 .
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
Thus \(a\mapsto\pi_x(a)\) is integrable on \(B\).  The nonnegativity part of \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law} and the zero integral therefore yield
\[
\pi_x(a)=0
\quad\text{for Lebesgue-a.e. }a\in B.
\]% lean: setIntegral_eq_zero_iff_of_nonneg_ae
The point \(a=0\) has Lebesgue measure zero.  For Lebesgue-a.e. \(a\in B\) with \(a\neq0\), the inclusion \(B\subset[0,1]\) gives \(a>0\).  The lower envelope in \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning} and \(c_->0\) then give
\[
\pi_x(a)\geq c_-a^\kappa>0
\quad\text{for Lebesgue-a.e. }a\in B\setminus\{0\}.
\]% lean: PolynomialThinning
Combining this positivity with \(\pi_x=0\) Lebesgue-a.e. on \(B\) gives
\[
\operatorname{Leb}(B)=0.
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique
Consequently \(f_1=f_2\) Lebesgue-a.e. on \(\mathbb R\), and the definitions of \(f_1\) and \(f_2\) give
\[
\mu_1(x,a)=\mu_2(x,a)
\quad\text{for Lebesgue-a.e. }a\in[0,\bar\delta].
\]% lean: cont_regression_extension_unique

Finally, both functions \(a\mapsto\mu_1(x,a)\) and \(a\mapsto\mu_2(x,a)\) are continuous on \([0,\bar\delta]\).  If they differed at some point of \([0,\bar\delta]\), continuity would make them differ on a nonempty relatively open interval in \([0,\bar\delta]\), which has positive Lebesgue measure, contradicting the almost-everywhere equality on \([0,\bar\delta]\).  Hence
\[
\mu_1(x,a)=\mu_2(x,a)
\qquad\text{for every }a\in[0,\bar\delta].
\]% lean: volume.eqOn_Icc_of_ae_eq
Since \(x\) was arbitrary, the conclusion holds in every stratum.
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:continuity-regression-extension-unique} justifies the pointwise notation for the continuity-only regression extension used in \cref{obj:def:continuity-clamp-functional}. The positive treatment-density envelope gives enough support throughout \([0,\bar\delta]\) for almost-everywhere equality of continuous versions to determine the extension at every threshold value.

\paragraph{Reproducing the verification.}
The material needed to reproduce the checks is organized as follows.

\emph{Archived source.}  The Lean development is released in the public CausalSmith repository at \url{https://github.com/Jiyuan-Tan/CausalSmith}; the state checked for this manuscript is the commit recorded above, and a snapshot of that commit is deposited in a persistent archive with a DOI at publication so that the checked state remains citable independently of the repository history.  All declarations for this paper live in the single module tree \texttt{CausalSmith/Stat/STAT\_LmtpThresholdAtomFrontier\_Research}.

\emph{Dependencies.}  The build needs the Lean toolchain \texttt{leanprover/lean4:v4.33.0}, pinned by the repository's \texttt{lean-toolchain} file and installed by \texttt{elan}, together with the Lean build tool \texttt{lake}, which is distributed with the toolchain.  The only non-Lean prerequisites are \texttt{git} and \texttt{curl}.  There are two Lean package dependencies, both pinned in the repository's lockfile: the Causalean library, on which this development builds, and Mathlib, on which Causalean builds.  No proprietary component, no external solver, and no network access beyond the initial fetch is required.

\emph{Build and check.}  From a clean environment, cloning the repository, checking out the recorded commit, fetching the pinned dependencies with \texttt{lake exe cache get}, and building the module tree above with \texttt{lake build} reproduces the kernel checks.  A successful build is not by itself the claim made here: the reported status additionally requires that the sources contain no \texttt{sorry}, no \texttt{admit}, and no added \texttt{axiom}, and that \texttt{\#print axioms} on each anchored declaration reports only the three standard Lean axioms.  Both checks are scripted in the repository and are rerun against the archived commit.

\emph{Anchor-to-declaration manifest.}  The bundle accompanying this manuscript ships a machine-readable crosswalk that maps every numbered environment in the paper to the declaration that certifies it.  Each record carries the manuscript anchor, the environment type and displayed number, the fully qualified Lean name, the declarations it uses, and a status field separating kernel-checked anchors from the presentation-synthesized restatements listed above, which carry no standalone declaration.  A companion file records, for each anchor, the verbatim source text of the certifying declaration, so a reader can compare a displayed statement with the checked one without rebuilding the library.  The interactive edition of this paper renders that manifest inline at each numbered environment.


\section{Proofs of the main results}\label{sec:deferred-proofs}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:minimax-risk}]
Fix the displayed regime constants.  We use seven steps.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Write \(R_n^\star\) for the observed minimax risk over the admissible estimator class used here,
\[
R_n^\star
=
\inf_{T_n}
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left[
\left|T_n-\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\right|
\right],
\]
where the infimum ranges over observed-sample measurable estimators satisfying
\(T_n(z)\in[0,1]\) for every observed sample \(z\).  For a split \(B_n\), put
\[
\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{TG}}(B_n)
=
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left[
\left|\widehat\theta_{n,B_n}^{\mathrm{TG}}-\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\right|
\right].
\]
This is the finite-sample stabilized risk in \cref{obj:synth_5}, with the product
sample law interpreted as in \cref{obj:synth_16}.  \Cref{obj:lem:stabilized-risk-upper}
gives a constant \(C_{\mathrm{up}}>0\) such that, for every admissible deterministic
\((\delta_n)\) and \(B_\bullet\), for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{TG}}(B_n)
\le
C_{\mathrm{up}}
\left(n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\right).
\]
The estimator \(\widehat\theta_{n,B_n}^{\mathrm{TG}}\) is observed-sample measurable and
\([0,1]\)-valued. Hence it is an admissible competitor in the infimum defining
\(R_n^\star\), so
\[
R_n^\star\le \mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{TG}}(B_n).
\]
% lean: observedMinimaxRisk_le_stabilizedWorstRisk

\item We record the two-point device used for both lower bounds.  Let
\(H_0,H_1\in\mathcal M\), put \(\vartheta_j=\theta_\delta(H_j)\), and suppose
\[
0\le \Delta\le |\vartheta_1-\vartheta_0|,
\qquad
0\le\delta\le1,
\]
with well-posed product chi-squared divergence
\[
H_1^{\otimes n}\ll H_0^{\otimes n},
\qquad
\int\left(\frac{dH_1^{\otimes n}}{dH_0^{\otimes n}}-1\right)^2dH_0^{\otimes n}<\infty,
\]
and
\[
\chi^2(H_1^{\otimes n},H_0^{\otimes n})\le \chi,
\qquad
0\le\chi<4 .
\]
For any admissible \([0,1]\)-valued observed-sample estimator \(T_n\), the testing
inequality and
\(\|H_1^{\otimes n}-H_0^{\otimes n}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le \frac12\sqrt{\chi}\) give
\[
\max_{j=0,1}
H_j^{\otimes n}
\left\{
|T_n-\vartheta_j|\ge \Delta/2
\right\}
\ge
\frac{1-\frac12\sqrt{\chi}}{2}.
\]
Multiplying by \(\Delta/2\), the larger of the two risks under \(H_0,H_1\) is at least
\[
\frac{1-\frac12\sqrt{\chi}}{4}\,\Delta .
\]
The supremum over \(P\in\mathcal M\) in the restricted minimax problem dominates these
two risks for each \(T_n\); taking the infimum over admissible \(T_n\) yields
\[
R_n^\star
\ge
\frac{1-\frac12\sqrt{\chi}}{4}\,\Delta .
\]
% lean: observedMinimaxRisk_lower_of_two_point

\item For the parametric component, use the canonical design
\[
P(X=x)=1/J,
\qquad
A\mid X=x\sim (\kappa+1)a^\kappa\,da,
\quad a\in[0,1],
\]
and Bernoulli regressions
\[
\mu_{x}^{P_{\mathrm r,0}}(a)=\frac12,
\qquad
\mu_{x}^{P_{\mathrm r,1}}(a)=\frac12+\varepsilon_n,
\qquad
\varepsilon_n=\frac14 n^{-1/2}.
\]
Both laws belong to \(\mathcal M\).  The stratum masses are \(1/J\), which meets the stratum-mass condition of \cref{obj:ass:stratum-mass} under the regime constraint \(p_{\min}\le1/J\); the canonical density \((\kappa+1)a^\kappa\) meets the conditional-density condition of \cref{obj:ass:conditional-density-law} and, by the regime constraint \(c_-\le\kappa+1\le c_+\), the polynomial-thinning envelope of \cref{obj:ass:polynomial-thinning}; and each regression is constant, so every Taylor remainder in \cref{obj:ass:holder-regression} vanishes and the Hölder condition holds for any \(L>0\).  Finally \(|\varepsilon_n|\le1/4\) for all \(n\ge1\), so both constant regressions take values in \([0,1]\).
The clamp target shifts by
\[
\left|
\theta_{\delta_n}(P_{\mathrm r,1})
-
\theta_{\delta_n}(P_{\mathrm r,0})
\right|
=\varepsilon_n .
\]
The product chi-squared divergence is well posed, and
\[
1+\chi^2(P_{\mathrm r,1}^{\otimes n},P_{\mathrm r,0}^{\otimes n})
=
(1+4\varepsilon_n^2)^n
\le
\exp(n\,4\varepsilon_n^2)
=
\exp(1/4).
\]
With \(\chi_{\mathrm r}=\exp(1/4)-1<4\), the two-point mixture bound \((1+4\varepsilon_n^2)^n\le\exp(1/4)\) gives a constant
\(c_{\mathrm r}>0\), depending only on the regime constants, such that
\[
c_{\mathrm r}n^{-1/2}\le R_n^\star
\]
for every admissible \((\delta_n)\) and all sufficiently large \(n\).
% lean: observedMinimaxRisk_eventually_ge_root

\item For the localized lower-bound component, use a fixed \(C^\infty\) dose bump
\(b_\circ:\mathbb R\to[0,1]\) centered at zero, with
\[
b_\circ(0)=1,
\qquad
0\le b_\circ(u)\le1,
\qquad
b_\circ(u)=0\quad\text{for }u\notin[-1,1].
\]
Let \(\ell=\ell(\beta)\).  Since \(\beta>0\), the number
\(\gamma_\beta=\beta-\ell\) belongs to \((0,1]\).  Because \(b_\circ\) is smooth and
compactly supported, there is a finite constant \(K_{\mathrm{bump}}\ge1\) such that
\[
\left|
b_\circ^{(\ell)}(u)-b_\circ^{(\ell)}(v)
\right|
\le
K_{\mathrm{bump}}|u-v|^{\gamma_\beta},
\qquad u,v\in\mathbb R.
\]
Choose \(a_{\mathrm{low}}>0\) so small that
\[
a_{\mathrm{low}}\le\frac14,
\qquad
a_{\mathrm{low}}K_{\mathrm{bump}}\le L .
\]
For any \(0\le\delta\le1\) and \(0<h\le1\), define
\[
q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(a)
=
a_{\mathrm{low}}h^\beta
b_\circ\!\left(\frac{a-\delta}{h}\right),
\qquad
f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(a)
=
\frac12+q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(a).
\]
Then \(f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}\) is continuous on \([0,1]\), and
\(f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(a)\in[0,1]\) there because
\[
0\le q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(a)\le a_{\mathrm{low}}h^\beta\le a_{\mathrm{low}}\le\frac14 .
\]
For \(s,t\in[0,1]\), differentiating the scaled bump \(\ell\) times and using
\(\gamma_\beta=\beta-\ell\) gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left|
\frac{d^\ell}{da^\ell}q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(s)
-
\frac{d^\ell}{da^\ell}q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(t)
\right|
&\le
 a_{\mathrm{low}}h^{\beta-\ell}K_{\mathrm{bump}}
 \left|\frac{s-\delta}{h}-\frac{t-\delta}{h}\right|^{\gamma_\beta} \\
&=
 a_{\mathrm{low}}K_{\mathrm{bump}}|s-t|^{\beta-\ell}
\le
 L|s-t|^{\beta-\ell}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The one-dimensional Taylor remainder theorem on \([0,1]\) therefore yields
\[
\left|
f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}}(t)
-
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
\frac{(f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{low}})^{(j)}(s)}{j!}(t-s)^j
\right|
\le
L|t-s|^\beta,
\qquad s,t\in[0,1].
\]
Thus the localized Bernoulli regression satisfies the member condition in
\cref{obj:ass:holder-regression} uniformly over \(0\le\delta\le1\) and \(0<h\le1\).
Set
\[
\zeta_{\mathrm{low}}=8(\kappa+1)a_{\mathrm{low}}^2,
\qquad
\rho_\beta=2\beta+1,
\qquad
\tau_{\mathrm{low}}
=
\exp\!\left(-\frac{\zeta_{\mathrm{low}}+1}{\rho_\beta}\right).
\]
Then \(0<\tau_{\mathrm{low}}\le1\) and
\[
\zeta_{\mathrm{low}}\tau_{\mathrm{low}}^{2\beta+1}\le1.
\]
For the contracted bandwidth \(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n\), form Bernoulli laws
\[
\mu_x^{Q_{\mathrm{low},0}}(a)=\frac12,
\qquad
\mu_x^{Q_{\mathrm{low},1}}(a)=\frac12+
 a_{\mathrm{low}}(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^\beta
 b_\circ\!\left(\frac{a-\delta_n}{\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n}\right)
\]
on the same canonical design.  For all sufficiently large \(n\),
\cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance} gives
\[
0<h_n,
\qquad
h_n\le1-\bar\delta,
\qquad
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
Together with \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\), this gives \(\delta_n\in[0,1]\),
\(0<\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n\le h_n\le1\), and \(\delta_n+h_n\le1\).  The displayed
Taylor bound places both laws in \(\mathcal M\).  The target separation obeys
\[
a_{\mathrm{low}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^\beta
\le
\left|
\theta_{\delta_n}(Q_{\mathrm{low},1})
-
\theta_{\delta_n}(Q_{\mathrm{low},0})
\right|.
\]
The one-observation chi-squared increment satisfies
\[
\int 4a_{\mathrm{low}}^2(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^{2\beta}
 b_\circ\!\left(\frac{a-\delta_n}{\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n}\right)^2
\,dP_{X,A}(x,a)
\le
8(\kappa+1)a_{\mathrm{low}}^2
(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^\kappa .
\]
Using the balance identity above, \(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n\le h_n\), and \(\kappa\ge0\),
\[
n\int 4a_{\mathrm{low}}^2(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^{2\beta}
 b_\circ\!\left(\frac{a-\delta_n}{\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n}\right)^2
\,dP_{X,A}(x,a)
\le
\zeta_{\mathrm{low}}\tau_{\mathrm{low}}^{2\beta+1}
\le1 .
\]
Let
\[
I_{\mathrm{low},n}
=
\int 4a_{\mathrm{low}}^2(\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n)^{2\beta}
 b_\circ\!\left(\frac{a-\delta_n}{\tau_{\mathrm{low}}h_n}\right)^2
\,dP_{X,A}(x,a)
\]
be the one-observation chi-squared increment for this Bernoulli perturbation.  The product law factorization gives
\[
1+\chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{low},1}^{\otimes n},Q_{\mathrm{low},0}^{\otimes n})
=
(1+I_{\mathrm{low},n})^n .
\]
Since \(I_{\mathrm{low},n}\ge0\) and \(nI_{\mathrm{low},n}\le1\), the inequality \(1+u\le e^u\) yields
\[
\chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{low},1}^{\otimes n},Q_{\mathrm{low},0}^{\otimes n})
\le
\exp(nI_{\mathrm{low},n})-1
\le e-1<4 .
\]
Applying the two-point bound gives a constant \(c_{\mathrm{loc}}>0\) such that
\[
c_{\mathrm{loc}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta
\le
R_n^\star
\]
for every admissible \((\delta_n)\) and all sufficiently large \(n\).
% lean: observedMinimaxRisk_eventually_ge_local

\item The global witness in the theorem uses the same canonical design and
\[
\mu_x^{P_0}(a)=\frac12,
\qquad
\mu_x^{P_1}(a)=\frac12+n^{-1/2}.
\]
For all sufficiently large \(n\), \(n^{-1/2}\le1/2\), so these are Bernoulli laws in
\(\mathcal M\), with shared \((X,A)\)-design, shared \(p_x\), and shared \(\pi_x\).
They satisfy
\[
\mu_x^{P_1}(a)=\mu_x^{P_0}(a)+n^{-1/2}
\]
for every \(x\) and \(a\in[0,1]\), and
\[
\frac12 n^{-1/2}
\le
\left|
\theta_{\delta_n}(P_1)-\theta_{\delta_n}(P_0)
\right|.
\]
The one-observation chi-squared increment is \(4n^{-1}\).  Hence the product divergence is
well posed and satisfies
\[
1+\chi^2(P_1^{\otimes n},P_0^{\otimes n})
=
(1+4n^{-1})^n
\le
\exp(4),
\]
so
\[
\chi^2(P_1^{\otimes n},P_0^{\otimes n})
\le
\exp(4).
\]
% lean: globalBernoulli_witness_eventually

\item For the localized witness appearing in the statement, use the same fixed bump
\(b_\circ\).  The amplitude construction above also permits a separate witness
amplitude: choose \(\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}>0\) such that
\[
\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}\le\frac14,
\qquad
\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}K_{\mathrm{bump}}\le L .
\]
For every \(0\le\delta\le1\) and \(0<h\le1\), the function
\[
f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{wit}}(t)
=
\frac12+\eta_{\mathrm{wit}} h^\beta b_\circ\!\left(\frac{t-\delta}{h}\right)
\]
is continuous on \([0,1]\), takes values in \([0,1]\), and the same scaled-derivative
calculation gives
\[
\left|
f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{wit}}(t)
-
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
\frac{(f_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{wit}})^{(j)}(s)}{j!}(t-s)^j
\right|
\le
L|t-s|^\beta,
\qquad s,t\in[0,1].
\]
Set
\[
\zeta_{\mathrm{wit}}=8(\kappa+1)\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}^2,
\qquad
D_{\mathrm{wit}}=\exp(\zeta_{\mathrm{wit}}).
\]
For \(h_n\) itself, define
\[
\mu_x^{Q_0}(t)=\frac12,
\qquad
\mu_x^{Q_1}(t)=\frac12+
 \eta_{\mathrm{wit}} h_n^\beta b_\circ\!\left(\frac{t-\delta_n}{h_n}\right).
\]
For all sufficiently large \(n\), \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance} gives
\[
0<h_n,
\qquad
h_n\le1-\bar\delta,
\qquad
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
Together with \(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\), this gives \(\delta_n\in[0,1]\),
\(h_n\le1\), and \(\delta_n+h_n\le1\), so \(Q_0,Q_1\in\mathcal M\).  The pair has
Bernoulli outcomes, common design, common \(p_x\), common \(\pi_x\), and
\[
\mu_x^{Q_1}(t)-\mu_x^{Q_0}(t)
=
\eta_{\mathrm{wit}} h_n^\beta b_\circ\!\left(\frac{t-\delta_n}{h_n}\right)
\]
for every \(x\) and \(t\in[0,1]\).  Since \(b_\circ(0)=1\) and the perturbation is
nonnegative,
\[
\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta
\le
\left|
\theta_{\delta_n}(Q_1)-\theta_{\delta_n}(Q_0)
\right|.
\]
The same integral calculation and the balance identity give
\[
n\int 4\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}^2h_n^{2\beta}
 b_\circ\!\left(\frac{t-\delta_n}{h_n}\right)^2
\,dP_{X,A}(x,t)
\le
\zeta_{\mathrm{wit}}.
\]
Let
\[
I_{\mathrm{wit},n}
=
\int 4\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}^2h_n^{2\beta}
 b_\circ\!\left(\frac{t-\delta_n}{h_n}\right)^2
\,dP_{X,A}(x,t)
\]
be the one-observation chi-squared increment.  The product law factorization gives
\[
1+\chi^2(Q_1^{\otimes n},Q_0^{\otimes n})
=
(1+I_{\mathrm{wit},n})^n .
\]
The perturbation is bounded by \(1/4\), so the Bernoulli likelihood ratio is finite and
square-integrable for one observation; absolute continuity and square integrability tensorize to
the product laws, giving a well-posed product chi-squared divergence.  Since
\(I_{\mathrm{wit},n}\ge0\) and \(nI_{\mathrm{wit},n}\le\zeta_{\mathrm{wit}}\), the inequality
\(1+u\le e^u\) gives
\[
\chi^2(Q_1^{\otimes n},Q_0^{\otimes n})
\le
\exp(nI_{\mathrm{wit},n})-1
\le
\exp(\zeta_{\mathrm{wit}})-1
\le
D_{\mathrm{wit}} .
\]
% lean: localizedBernoulli_witness_eventually

\item Now set
\[
c=
\frac12\min\{c_{\mathrm r},c_{\mathrm{loc}},1/2,\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}\},
\qquad
C=C_{\mathrm{up}}+D_{\mathrm{wit}}+\exp(4)+c+1 .
\]
The amplitude asserted in the theorem is \(\eta_{\mathrm{wit}}\).  Then \(0<c<C\), and \(c\le1/2\), \(c\le \eta_{\mathrm{wit}}\), \(2c\le c_{\mathrm r}\), and
\(2c\le c_{\mathrm{loc}}\).  Increasing the eventual index so that all preceding
bounds hold, put
\[
r_{0,n}=n^{-1/2},
\qquad
r_{1,n}=\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta,
\qquad
r_n=r_{0,n}+r_{1,n}.
\]
The two lower components imply
\[
R_n^\star\ge c_{\mathrm r}r_{0,n},
\qquad
R_n^\star\ge c_{\mathrm{loc}}r_{1,n}.
\]
Since \(r_{0,n},r_{1,n}\ge0\), these inequalities and the choices of \(c\) give
\[
R_n^\star
\ge
\max\{2c\,r_{0,n},2c\,r_{1,n}\}
\ge
\frac12(2c\,r_{0,n})+
\frac12(2c\,r_{1,n})
=
c r_n .
\]
Together with the admissibility and upper bounds above,
\[
c r_n
\le
R_n^\star
\le
\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{TG}}(B_n)
\le
C r_n .
\]
By \cref{obj:synth_5,obj:synth_16}, \(\mathcal R_n^{\mathrm{TG}}(B_n)\) is the
finite-sample worst-case risk \(\operatorname{Risk}_n(B_n)\) of the stabilized estimator
computed under the observed i.i.d. product law.  The global witness constructed above satisfies
\[
c n^{-1/2}
\le
\left|\theta_{\delta_n}(P_1)-\theta_{\delta_n}(P_0)\right|,
\qquad
\chi^2(P_1^{\otimes n},P_0^{\otimes n})\le C,
\]
because \(c\le1/2\) and \(\exp(4)\le C\).  The localized witness constructed above
satisfies
\[
c\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta
\le
\left|\theta_{\delta_n}(Q_1)-\theta_{\delta_n}(Q_0)\right|,
\qquad
\chi^2(Q_1^{\otimes n},Q_0^{\otimes n})\le C,
\]
because \(c\le \eta_{\mathrm{wit}}\) and \(D_{\mathrm{wit}}\le C\).  These are the asserted eventual
bounds and witness properties.
% lean: clamp_minimax_risk
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:honest-length}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Let \(\ell=\lceil\beta\rceil-1\) and
\[
t_\alpha=\sqrt{\frac{\log(12J/\alpha)}{2}} .
\]
The expected-length upper bound used below is the following eventual statement: there is a constant
\(C_{\mathrm{up}}>0\), depending only on the declared regime constants, such that for every admissible
threshold path and split path,
\[
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\!\left[\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n)\right]
\le
C_{\mathrm{up}}
\left(n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\right)
\]
for all sufficiently large \(n\). To see this, use \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}. Intersecting the
symmetric interval with \([0,1]\) weakly shortens its length, and therefore
\[
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n)\right]
\le
2\,\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[
 b_{0,n}+\sum_{x\in\mathcal X}D_{n,x}\right],
\]
where
\[
b_{0,n}=\frac{t_\alpha}{\sqrt{|I_{0,n}|}},
\qquad
b_{1,n}=\frac{t_\alpha}{\sqrt{|I_{1,n}|}},
\]
and \(D_{n,x}\) is the stratum contribution in \cref{obj:def:honest-interval}. The split lower bounds
in \cref{obj:synth_8} give \(b_{0,n},b_{1,n}\le 3t_\alpha n^{-1/2}\) once \(n\ge8\).

For a fixed law \(P\in\mathcal M\) and stratum \(x\), the integrated stratum contribution is bounded by
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}D_{n,x}
&\le
\left(\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\widehat b_x+b_{1,n}\right)
\left[ C_{\ell,1}Lh_n^\beta
+t_\alpha
\int
\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2,n}}w_{ix}(z)^2\right)^{1/2}
\,dP^{\otimes n}(z)
\right]
+b_{1,n}+P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c) .
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(C_{\ell,1}\) is the finite \(\ell_1\)-weight constant supplied on \(\Omega_x\) by
\cref{obj:lem:total-gram}. The displayed product bound is the sample-splitting step: the empirical atom
estimate uses \(I_{1,n}\), the local weights use \(I_{2,n}\), and the mixed term factors as
\[
\int \widehat b_x(z)
\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2,n}}w_{ix}(z)^2\right)^{1/2}
\,dP^{\otimes n}(z)
=
\left(\int\widehat b_x\,dP^{\otimes n}\right)
\left(\int
\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2,n}}w_{ix}^2\right)^{1/2}
\,dP^{\otimes n}\right).
\]
For the empirical atom envelope, define
\[
f_x(O)=\mathbf 1\{X=x,\ A\leq\delta_n\},
\qquad
m_x(P)=p_xq_x(\delta_n).
\]
Then \(\widehat b_x=|I_{1,n}|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{1,n}}f_x(O_i)\). The mean of one summand is
\[
\mathbb E_P f_x(O)
=P\{X=x,A\leq\delta_n\}
=p_x\int_0^{\delta_n}\pi_x(a)\,da
=p_xq_x(\delta_n)
=m_x(P).
\]
The second equality is the conditional-density component of \cref{obj:def:model-class}, and the third is the definition of \(q_x\) in \cref{obj:def:clamp-functional}.
Since \(0\leq f_x\leq1\), \(\operatorname{Var}_P\{f_x(O)\}\leq1/4\). Independence on the deterministic second block therefore yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}
\left|\widehat b_x-m_x(P)\right|
&\leq
\left\{\operatorname{Var}_{P^{\otimes n}}(\widehat b_x)\right\}^{1/2}  \\
&\leq
\frac12 |I_{1,n}|^{-1/2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Moreover, \cref{obj:prop:pushforward-setup} gives
\(q_x(\delta_n)\leq c_+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}/(\kappa+1)\), and \(p_x\leq1\), so
\[
m_x(P)\leq \frac{c_+}{\kappa+1}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}.
\]
The triangle inequality gives the integrated envelope
\[
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\widehat b_x
\le
\frac12 |I_{1,n}|^{-1/2}
+
\frac{c_+}{\kappa+1}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}.
\]
Also, \cref{obj:lem:weight-energy-balance} gives
\[
\int
\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2,n}}w_{ix}(z)^2\right)^{1/2}
\,dP^{\otimes n}(z)
\le C_{\mathrm{eng}}h_n^\beta
\]
for a finite constant \(C_{\mathrm{eng}}\), and \cref{obj:lem:total-gram} gives
\[
P^{\otimes n}(\Omega_x^c)
\le
C_{\mathrm{tg}}\exp\{-c_{\mathrm{tg}}n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\}.
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance}, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1,
\qquad
0<h_n,
\qquad
h_n\le1.
\]
The effective local sample size has the following lower bound. Put
\[
\gamma_{\mathrm{bal}}=2\beta+\kappa+1 .
\]
Since \(\delta_n\ge0\), \(h_n>0\), and \(\kappa\ge0\),
\[
h_n^{\gamma_{\mathrm{bal}}}
=h_n^{2\beta+1}h_n^\kappa
\le h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa
=n^{-1}.
\]
Because \(\gamma_{\mathrm{bal}}>0\), this gives \(h_n\le n^{-1/\gamma_{\mathrm{bal}}}\). Multiplying the balance identity by \(h_n^{-2\beta}\) gives
\[
n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=h_n^{-2\beta},
\]
and therefore, since \(-2\beta<0\),
\[
n^{2\beta/(2\beta+\kappa+1)}
=\left(n^{-1/\gamma_{\mathrm{bal}}}\right)^{-2\beta}
\le h_n^{-2\beta}
=n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa .
\]
Exponential decay along this polynomial scale is eventually bounded by \(n^{-1/2}\). Combining the
exponential-decay bound with the polynomial scale bound for \(n h_n(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa\), and using
\(h_n^\beta\le1\), yields uniformly in \(P\in\mathcal M\) and \(x\in\mathcal X\),
\[
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}D_{n,x}
\le
C_{\mathrm{rad}}
\left(n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\right).
\]
Since \(J\) is fixed, summing over strata gives the displayed \(C_{\mathrm{up}}\) bound.

\item Put \(\eta=1-2\alpha>0\). The lower-bound input is a two-point inequality for expected length.  Because an arbitrary interval procedure need not have finite expected length, the worst-case length in this step is taken in \([0,\infty]\), with \(\int^{-}\) denoting the lower integral of a nonnegative measurable function; for the bounded intervals of interest this is the ordinary expected length.
If \(P_0,P_1\in\mathcal M\) satisfy
\[
\Delta\le |\theta_{\delta_n}(P_1)-\theta_{\delta_n}(P_0)|,
\qquad
P_1^{\otimes n}\ll P_0^{\otimes n},
\]
the likelihood-ratio square is \(P_0^{\otimes n}\)-integrable, and
\(\chi^2(P_1^{\otimes n},P_0^{\otimes n})\le\chi\), then every uniformly honest observed-sample
interval procedure \(C_n\) satisfies
\[
\max\!\left\{\left(1-2\alpha-\frac12\sqrt\chi\right)\Delta,0\right\}
\le
\sup\left\{
\int^{-}\operatorname{len}(C_n)\,dP^{\otimes n}: P\in\mathcal M
\right\},
\]
where the supremum and the lower integral are taken in \([0,\infty]\) and the nonnegative real number on the left is read as an element of \([0,\infty]\) as in \cref{obj:synth_11}. The comparisons in this step are made in \([0,\infty]\); the final paragraph returns to the ordinary expected-length notation of \cref{obj:synth_3,obj:synth_17} once the interval is known to be bounded. Indeed, coverage at \(P_0\) and
\(P_1\), transferred from \(P_1^{\otimes n}\) to \(P_0^{\otimes n}\) by
\[
d_{\mathrm{TV}}(P_1^{\otimes n},P_0^{\otimes n})
\le \frac12\sqrt{\chi},
\]
gives \(P_0^{\otimes n}\{\theta_{\delta_n}(P_0),\theta_{\delta_n}(P_1)\in C_n\}\ge
1-2\alpha-\frac12\sqrt\chi\). On that event the interval length is at least \(\Delta\), and integrating
this indicator gives the displayed extended-real bound.

For the root component, set
\[
\varepsilon_n=\tau_\alpha n^{-1/2},
\qquad
\tau_\alpha=\frac{\eta}{16},
\qquad
\chi_\alpha=\frac{\eta^2}{16}.
\]
Let \(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{root}}_{0,n}\) and \(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{root}}_{1,n}\) have the common design
\[
\mathbb P(X=x,A\in da)=\frac1J(\kappa+1)a^\kappa\mathbf 1\{0\leq a\leq1\}\,da,
\]
and, conditionally on \((X,A)=(x,a)\), let \(Y\) be Bernoulli with success probability
\[
\frac12+g^{\mathrm{root}}_{j,n}(x,a),
\qquad
g^{\mathrm{root}}_{0,n}(x,a)=0,\qquad
g^{\mathrm{root}}_{1,n}(x,a)=\varepsilon_n .
\]
For all sufficiently large \(n\), \(|\varepsilon_n|\leq1/2\). The two laws therefore have outcomes in \([0,1]\), the same density \(\pi_x(a)=(\kappa+1)a^\kappa\), the same masses \(p_x=1/J\), and constant Hölder regressions; the regime inequalities place both laws in \(\mathcal M\). Their clamp-target gap is exactly
\[
\begin{aligned}
\theta_{\delta_n}(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{root}}_{1,n})
-\theta_{\delta_n}(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{root}}_{0,n})
&=\varepsilon_n
\left[
\mathbb P(A>\delta_n)+\sum_{x\in\mathcal X}p_xq_x(\delta_n)
\right] \\
&=\varepsilon_n .
\end{aligned}
\]
For one observation the chi-square divergence is \(4\varepsilon_n^2\), hence independence gives
\[
1+\chi^2\!\left((\mathbb P^{\mathrm{root}}_{1,n})^{\otimes n},
(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{root}}_{0,n})^{\otimes n}\right)
=(1+4\varepsilon_n^2)^n.
\]
Since \(4n\varepsilon_n^2=\eta^2/64\),
\[
(1+4\varepsilon_n^2)^n
\le \exp(\eta^2/64)
\le 1+\eta^2/64+(\eta^2/64)^2
\le 1+\chi_\alpha .
\]
The two-point length inequality above, with \(\chi=\chi_\alpha\), yields
\[
\max\!\left\{
\left(\eta-\frac12\sqrt{\chi_\alpha}\right)\varepsilon_n,0
\right\}
\le L_n^\star .
\]
Thus, for some \(c_{\mathrm{root}}>0\),
\[
\max\!\left\{c_{\mathrm{root}}n^{-1/2},0\right\}
\le L_n^\star
\]
eventually.

For the local component, use the fixed compactly supported \(C^\infty\) bump \(\mathfrak b:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) from the same two-point construction, normalized by
\[
\mathfrak b(0)=1,
\qquad
0\leq\mathfrak b(u)\leq1,
\qquad
\mathfrak b(u)=0\ \text{whenever } |u|\geq1 .
\]
The smoothness calibration for this particular bump gives an amplitude
\(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}\in(0,1/4]\), depending only on \((\beta,L)\), with the following uniform property. For every \(0\leq\delta\leq1\) and \(0<h\leq1\), define
\[
q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{bump}}(a)
=
\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}h^\beta
\mathfrak b\!\left(\frac{a-\delta}{h}\right).
\]
Then \(a\mapsto 1/2+q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{bump}}(a)\) is continuous on \([0,1]\), takes values in \([0,1]\), and satisfies the Hölder Taylor remainder
\[
\left|
\left(\frac12+q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{bump}}(t)\right)
-
\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}
\frac{\partial_{[0,1]}^j(1/2+q_{\delta,h}^{\mathrm{bump}})(s)}{j!}(t-s)^j
\right|
\le L|t-s|^\beta
\]
for all \(s,t\in[0,1]\). Indeed, applying the normalized Hölder gate of \(\mathfrak b\) with radius \(\min\{L,1\}\) and then shrinking the amplitude to be at most \(1/4\) gives both the displayed Taylor bound and the range constraint.

Set
\[
\zeta_{\mathrm{loc}}
=
\frac{\eta^2/64}{8(\kappa+1)\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}^2+1},
\qquad
h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{loc}}h_n .
\]
Let \(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{loc}}_{0,n}\) and \(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{loc}}_{1,n}\) have the same common design as the root pair, and let their conditional Bernoulli success probabilities be \(1/2+g^{\mathrm{loc}}_{j,n}(x,a)\), where
\[
g^{\mathrm{loc}}_{0,n}(x,a)=0,\qquad
g^{\mathrm{loc}}_{1,n}(x,a)
=\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}^\beta
\mathfrak b\!\left(\frac{a-\delta_n}{h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}}\right).
\]
For all sufficiently large \(n\), \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance} gives \(0<h_n\le1\), hence \(0<h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}\le1\), and the amplitude calibration places both laws in \(\mathcal M\). The localized shift is nonnegative and equals \(\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}^\beta\) at \(a=\delta_n\), so the collapsed atom contribution alone gives
\[
\left|
\theta_{\delta_n}(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{loc}}_{1,n})
-\theta_{\delta_n}(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{loc}}_{0,n})
\right|
\geq
\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}^\beta
=
\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}\zeta_{\mathrm{loc}}^\beta
\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta .
\]
For one observation, write
\[
\mathcal I_n
=
\int 4\{g^{\mathrm{loc}}_{1,n}(x,a)\}^2\,d\mathbb P_{X,A}(x,a),
\]
where \(\mathbb P_{X,A}\) is the common design law. Since \(0\leq\mathfrak b\leq1\) and \(\mathfrak b\) vanishes outside \([-1,1]\),
\[
\mathcal I_n
\le
8(\kappa+1)\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}^2
h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_{n,\mathrm{loc}})^\kappa .
\]
Because \(0<\zeta_{\mathrm{loc}}\leq1\), \(h_{n,\mathrm{loc}}\leq h_n\). With \(2\beta+1>1\), \(\kappa\geq0\), and the balance identity from \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance},
\[
\begin{aligned}
n\mathcal I_n
&\le
8(\kappa+1)\mathfrak a_{\mathrm{bump}}^2
\zeta_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2\beta+1}
\,n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa \\
&\le
\eta^2/64 .
\end{aligned}
\]
Independence gives
\[
1+\chi^2\!\left((\mathbb P^{\mathrm{loc}}_{1,n})^{\otimes n},
(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{loc}}_{0,n})^{\otimes n}\right)
=(1+\mathcal I_n)^n
\le \exp(\eta^2/64)
\le 1+\chi_\alpha .
\]
The two-point length inequality therefore gives, for some \(c_{\mathrm{loc}}>0\),
\[
\max\!\left\{c_{\mathrm{loc}}\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta,0\right\}
\le L_n^\star
\]
eventually. Taking half the smaller of \(c_{\mathrm{root}}\) and \(c_{\mathrm{loc}}\), and splitting into
the two cases according to which component is larger, gives a constant \(c_{\mathrm{min}}>0\) such that
\[
\max\!\left\{c_{\mathrm{min}}\left(n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\right),0\right\}
\le L_n^\star
\]
eventually.

The every-procedure version uses that a uniformly honest procedure is an admissible competitor in the
minimax honest-length criterion. Hence there is a positive constant \(c_{\mathrm{proc}}>0\) such that
every uniformly honest observed-sample interval procedure \(C_n\) satisfies
\[
\max\!\left\{c_{\mathrm{proc}}\left(n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\right),0\right\}
\le
\sup\left\{
\int^{-}\operatorname{len}(C_n)\,dP^{\otimes n}: P\in\mathcal M
\right\}
\]
in \(\mathbb R_{\ge0}\cup\{\infty\}\).

\item Define
\[
c=\frac12\min\{c_{\mathrm{min}},c_{\mathrm{proc}}\},
\qquad
C=C_{\mathrm{up}}+c+1 .
\]
Then \(c>0\), \(c<C\), \(c\le c_{\mathrm{min}}\), \(c\le c_{\mathrm{proc}}\), and \(C_{\mathrm{up}}\le C\).

\item Fix an admissible threshold path \((\delta_n)\) and split path \(B_\bullet\). By
\cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance}, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
0<h_n,
\qquad
h_n\le 1-\bar\delta,
\qquad
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
Together with \(0\le\delta_n\le\bar\delta\), this gives \(\delta_n+h_n\le1\). Hence the hypotheses of
\cref{obj:lem:stabilized-interval-honesty} hold. That result supplies observed-sample measurability of
\(\mathrm{CI}_n\) and the modelwise coverage inequality
\[
1-\alpha
\le
P^{\otimes n}\{\theta_{\delta_n}(P)\in \mathrm{CI}_n\}
\]
for every \(P\in\mathcal M\), so the stabilized interval is uniformly honest.
Taking the infimum over \(P\) gives the displayed worst-case coverage bound.

\item Put
\[
r_n=n^{-1/2}+\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta .
\]
Since \(n^{-1/2}>0\) and \(\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta\ge0\), we have \(r_n>0\). The upper ingredient and \(C_{\mathrm{up}}\le C\)
yield
\[
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n)\right]
\le C r_n .
\]
The minimax lower ingredient and \(c\le c_{\mathrm{min}}\) yield the extended-real comparison
\[
\max\{c r_n,0\}\le L_n^\star .
\]

Since \(\mathrm{CI}_n\) is itself uniformly honest by the coverage step, the procedure lower bound
applies to this concrete interval. Combined with \(c\le c_{\mathrm{proc}}\), it gives
\[
\max\{c r_n,0\}
\le
\sup\left\{
\int^{-}\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n)\,dP^{\otimes n}: P\in\mathcal M
\right\}
\]
in \([0,\infty]\). For the concrete stabilized interval the length is measurable, nonnegative, and
bounded by one, so its worst-case length in \([0,\infty]\) agrees with its ordinary worst-case
expected length, read in \([0,\infty]\) as in \cref{obj:synth_11}. Since \(c r_n>0\), this gives the
real lower bound
\[
c r_n
\le
\sup_{P\in\mathcal M}
\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\!\left[\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n)\right].
\]

Finally, for every uniformly honest observed-sample interval procedure \(C_n\), the procedure lower
bound gives the extended-real statement
\[
\max\{c r_n,0\}
\le
\sup\left\{
\int^{-}\operatorname{len}(C_n)\,dP^{\otimes n}: P\in\mathcal M
\right\},
\]
with the supremum taken in \(\mathbb R_{\ge0}\cup\{\infty\}\). This is the arbitrary-procedure worst-length criterion used in the comparison. For interval procedures whose pointwise lengths lie in \([0,1]\) as in \cref{obj:synth_17}, the nonnegative lower integral equals the ordinary expected length under the positive-part embedding of \cref{obj:synth_11}, and the display specializes to the corresponding real expected-length lower bound.
These are the eventual length comparisons established by the argument.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:thm:phase-diagram}]
Let
\[
\Gamma=\beta\kappa+2\beta+\kappa+1,
\qquad
\Xi=2\beta+\kappa+1,
\qquad
\zeta=\frac{\Gamma}{2\beta+1}.
\]
The regime assumptions give \(\Gamma>0\), \(\Xi>0\), and \(\zeta>0\).

\begin{enumerate}
\item The two deterministic scales satisfy
\[
\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}{\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}}
=
\frac{n^{-1/(2\Gamma)}}{n^{-1/\Xi}}
=
n^{1/\Xi-1/(2\Gamma)}
\]
for every \(n\ge1\).  The exponent is positive because
\[
2\Gamma-\Xi
=
2\beta\kappa+2\beta+\kappa+1>0.
\]
Hence \(n^{1/\Xi-1/(2\Gamma)}\to\infty\), which proves
\[
\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}{\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}}\to\infty .
\]
% lean: critical_scale_div_edge_scale_tendsto_top

\item Fix a deterministic threshold sequence \((\delta_n)\) with
\(\delta_n\in[0,\bar\delta]\).  Let \(h_n\) be the information-balance bandwidth
from \cref{obj:def:bandwidth}, define
\[
a_n=\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta,
\]
and let \(r_n\) be the frontier rate in \cref{obj:def:frontier}, so that
\(r_n=n^{-1/2}+a_n\).  By \cref{obj:lem:bandwidth-balance}, for all sufficiently
large \(n\),
\[
h_n>0,
\qquad
h_n\le 1-\bar\delta,
\qquad
n h_n^{2\beta+1}(\delta_n+h_n)^\kappa=1 .
\]
The phase comparisons below use this deterministic balance relation directly.
In the regular regime \(\delta_n/\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}\to0\), we also have,
for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\[
\frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}<1 .
\]
If \(\delta_n=0\), then \(a_n=0\) because \(\kappa+1>0\).  If \(\delta_n>0\), set
\[
\eta_n=\bigl(n\delta_n^\kappa\bigr)^{-1/(2\beta+1)} .
\]
Since \(\delta_n+h_n\ge\delta_n\) and \(\kappa\ge0\), the balance equation implies
\[
h_n\le \eta_n .
\]
Therefore
\[
a_n
=
\delta_n^{\kappa+1}h_n^\beta
\le
\delta_n^{\kappa+1}\eta_n^\beta .
\]
The exact power computation is
\[
\frac{\delta_n^{\kappa+1}\eta_n^\beta}{n^{-1/2}}
=
\left(\frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}\right)^\zeta
\le 1 .
\]
Thus \(0\le a_n\le n^{-1/2}\) eventually, and
\[
n^{-1/2}\le r_n=n^{-1/2}+a_n\le 2n^{-1/2}
\]
eventually.  Hence \(r_n\asymp n^{-1/2}\).
% lean: regular_frontier_asymp

\item Suppose \(\delta_n/\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}\to c_0\) with \(c_0>0\).
By the scale separation \(\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}/\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\to\infty\),
\[
\frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}}
=
\frac{\delta_n}{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}
\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{crit},n}}{\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}}
\to\infty .
\]
For this above-edge regime, the balance equation gives the closed-form bandwidth
order.  Indeed, for all sufficiently large \(n\),
\(\delta_n>0\) and \(\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\le\delta_n\).  With
\(\eta_n=(n\delta_n^\kappa)^{-1/(2\beta+1)}\), the balance equation gives
\(h_n\le \eta_n\).  The inequality \(\delta_{\mathrm{edge},n}\le\delta_n\) gives
\(\eta_n\le\delta_n\), and hence \(\delta_n+h_n\le2\delta_n\).  Substituting this
upper bound into the same balance equation yields
\[
2^{-\kappa/(2\beta+1)} \eta_n\le h_n\le \eta_n .
\]
Consequently
\[
h_n\asymp \bigl(n\delta_n^\kappa\bigr)^{-1/(2\beta+1)} .
\]
Multiplying by the nonnegative factor \(\delta_n^{\kappa+1}\) and raising the
bandwidth comparison to the positive power \(\beta\) gives
\[
a_n\asymp
\delta_n^{\kappa+1}
\left\{\bigl(n\delta_n^\kappa\bigr)^{-1/(2\beta+1)}\right\}^{\beta}.
\]
The normalized interior term satisfies
\[
\frac{
\delta_n^{\kappa+1}
\left\{\bigl(n\delta_n^\kappa\bigr)^{-1/(2\beta+1)}\right\}^{\beta}
}{n^{-1/2}}
=
